RESEARCH PROGRAMME

Quantum holography and
associative chiral Koszul duality
Ordered collisions, chiral quantum groups, and the relation between boundary operators
and bulk theories.
Last updated 16 November 2026

Associative chiral Koszul duality studies singular composition with the order of the inputs re‐
tained. Its constructions include coefficient systems on configurations, vertex algebras, quantum
vertex algebras, and module categories with fusion. Bar and cobar relate multiplication to coher‐
ent comultiplication. A realization by fields must also specify the collision maps, completion,
local vacuum, and module tensor product.
The programme has coefficient dualities, explicit field duals, algebraic bulk operations, and restricted fu‐
sion theorems. Several comparisons now hold on full algebraic state spaces or at every finite configuration
arity. Their hypotheses distinguish polynomial states, completed inputs, ordinary derived categories, and
categories defined by different weak equivalences.
The remaining questions compare independently defined constructions. They concern native collision
operations, common completed fusion domains, geometric bulk actions, and states prepared by physical
amplitudes. A complementary-level partner, a bar dual, and a vacuum endomorphism algebra retain sepa‐
rate definitions.
The table gives the available results and the unresolved comparison for each example. The questions be‐
low state the mathematical data needed to extend them. A partial theorem retains its positive domain
even when a stronger comparison fails.

How to read the status marks
Proved and independently checked

Conditional

An independently checked proof establishes exactly
the stated scope. Publication status and published
antecedents are separate.

The conclusion is proved under an explicitly named
hypothesis that remains unproved.

Open problem

Published

The stated construction or comparison remains
unresolved.

An existing result in the literature, with its author,
source and year given. Its hypotheses remain those
of the cited result.

Partly established

Not yet formulated

A restricted case, a related theorem or an obstruc‐
tion is established; the full mathematical question
represented by the column remains unresolved.

No precise version of this mathematical question is
formulated for the example.

Refuted as first posed

Proposed

The proposed form is ruled out by a proved coun‐
terexample or obstruction. Any replacement is stat‐
ed with its own hypotheses.

A proposed construction has a written argument,
with the stated unresolved proof obligations.

Siegel–Weil for the hyperbolic genus
Let 𝑉 be a strongly rational holomorphic vertex operator algebra over ℂ, with its conformal vector,
algebraic graded direct-sum states, a chosen Cartan subalgebra of 𝑉1 , strongly rational
Heisenberg commutant 𝐶, and associated positive-definite even lattice of rank 𝑟 ≥ 3. Write
𝑐 = 𝑐(𝑉), let ℋ(𝑉) be its hyperbolic genus, let 𝐺𝑊 be the finite image of the Cartan-preserving au‐
tomorphisms of 𝑊 on its associated lattice, and put 𝑚(𝑉) = ∑[𝑊] ∈ ℋ(𝑉) |𝐺𝑊 |−1 . For 𝑟 > 4 and 𝜏
in the upper half-plane,

Here 𝜂 is the Dedekind eta function and 𝜀 its standard multiplier, with metaplectic lifts when re‐
quired; the sum is absolutely and locally uniformly convergent. At 𝑟 = 3,4, multiply each sum‐
mand by Im⁡(𝛾𝜏)𝑡 , continue meromorphically from Re⁡𝑡 > 1 − 𝑟/4, and evaluate at 𝑡 = 0: the
standard section specified by the real Gaussian and finite lattice-coset characteristic functions is reg‐
ular there and has the same value, with the metaplectic convention at rank three. The proof uses the
positive nonvacuum commutant weights supplied by Dong–Sun–Wang’s 2026 preprint; assuming
those weights are strictly positive is an explicit alternative hypothesis. For this holomorphic class
this establishes, in the absolutely convergent range 𝑟 > 4, the character identity of Möller–
Rayhaun, arXiv:2408.07117 (2024), Conjecture 5.7; at 𝑟 = 3,4 the identity holds for the specified
continuation. The arithmetic Siegel–Weil theorems are known inputs.

Associative bar–cobar duality with finite arity filtrations
Let 𝑋 be a smooth separated finite-type curve over a characteristic-zero field 𝑘, and equip
𝒮𝑋 = 𝐷(𝑘) × ∏𝑛 ≥ 1 DMod⁡(𝑋 𝑛 ) with consecutive-cut convolution, using unbounded right alge‐

braic differential modules with quasi-coherent underlying modules and derived external tensors.
Bar and cobar give inverse equivalences between augmented coherent associative algebras with
𝐴0 ≃ 𝑘 and counital coaugmented coherent coassociative coalgebras with 𝐶0 ≃ 𝑘, including map‐
ping spaces; each reduced word at arity 𝑛 has length at most 𝑛, so no cohomological boundedness
assumption is needed. These equivalences restrict to algebras whose positive-arity coefficients van‐
ish on every separated cut and strong cut coalgebras. For such a coalgebra, Ω𝐶𝑛 ≃ 𝑅Γ𝑍𝑛 (𝐶𝑛 )[−1],
where 𝑍1 = 𝑋 and 𝑍𝑛 = ⋂𝑛𝑝 −= 11 ⋃𝑖 ≤ 𝑝 < 𝑗 Δ𝑖𝑗 for 𝑛 ≥ 2; in particular, 𝑍3 = Δ13 .

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

The spectral bar comparison on augmentation-generated categories
Let 𝐻 be an augmented dg bialgebra over a characteristic-zero field 𝑘, with strict dg coproduct and
a degree-zero locally nilpotent algebra derivation and coalgebra coderivation 𝑇 satisfying
[𝑑𝐻 , 𝑇] = 0 and 𝜀𝑇 = 0. Use the direct-sum normalized bar resolution, full-product Hom cochains
𝐶(𝑀) = 𝑅 Hom𝐻 ⁡(𝑘, 𝑀), and 𝐵 = 𝐶(𝑘); for 𝑠 ∈ 𝑘, the translated coproduct Δ𝑠 = (𝑒𝑠𝑇 ⊗ 1)Δ gives
a natural comparison 𝐶(𝑀) ⊗𝐋𝐵 Θ𝑠 𝐶(𝑁) ⟶ 𝐶(𝑀 ⊗𝑠 𝑁), where Θ𝑠 translates the bar inputs and
twists the second left 𝐵-action. This map is an equivalence whenever either input belongs to
thick(𝑘) ⊂ 𝐷(𝐻), and cochains identify thick(𝑘) with Perf(𝐵), with coherent translated products
and units. If the augmentation module 𝑘 is compact in 𝐷(𝐻), the equivalence extends to
Loc⁡(𝑘) ≃ 𝐷(𝐵); compactness alone does not make 𝑘 generate all of 𝐷(𝐻).
New assembly of known results · Antecedents: Adams (1960); Positselski (2011); Witherspoon (2017); Keller (2006).

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Formal higher cohomology on the Hilbert scheme
For 𝑁 ≥ 1, let 𝒟 be the Arakawa–Kuwabara–Möller formal vertex-superalgebra sheaf obtained by

framed 𝔤𝔩𝑁 BRST reduction of adjoint and fundamental symplectic bosons with fundamental
fermions on 𝑀𝑁 = Hilb𝑁 ⁡(ℂ2 ), over 𝑅 = ℂ[[ℏ]]: each of 𝒟 and 𝒟/ℏ𝑚 𝒟, 𝑚 ≥ 1, has nonzero
sheaf cohomology exactly in degrees 0, …, 𝑁 − 1. For 𝑁 ≥ 2, its normal-cup subalgebra generated by
the vacuum and translated determinant classes is (𝑅 ⊗ℂ Λ(𝑒0 , 𝑒1 , …))/(ℏ𝑒𝑟 , Λ≥𝑁 ), with only finite
sums and products in the generators. Here 𝑒𝑟 has sheaf degree one, intrinsic even parity and confor‐
mal weight 𝑟 + 1; increasing products of fewer than 𝑁 generators are nonzero, not ℏ-divisible, and
have independent nonzero images in every finite ℏ quotient. These positive-degree classes vanish af‐
ter taking the torus-finite part and then specializing ℏ = 1.
New · Antecedents: Arakawa–Kuwabara–Möller (2023, preprint); Malikov–Schechtman–Vaintrob (1999); Gorbounov–
Malikov–Schechtman (2005); Haiman (1998); Arakawa–Malikov (2009, preprint).

Minimal-model fusion with a finite-base fibre
Let 2 ≤ 𝑝 < 𝑞 be coprime, set 𝑐𝑝, 𝑞 = 1 − 6(𝑝 − 𝑞)2 /(𝑝𝑞), and let 𝒞𝑝, 𝑞 be the complex linear cate‐
gory of finite direct sums of simple 𝐿(𝑐𝑝, 𝑞 , 0)-modules, with the simple vacuum as tensor unit. If 𝐼
indexes its simple modules, the functor 𝐹(𝑀) = ⨁𝑖, 𝑗 ∈ 𝐼 Hom⁡(𝐿𝑖 , 𝑀 ⊠ 𝐿𝑗 ) is exact, faithful and

strong monoidal into finite ℂ𝐼 -bimodules, using tensor product over ℂ𝐼 . The finite weak Hopf al‐
gebra 𝐻 = ⨁𝑎 ∈ 𝐼 Endℂ ⁡(𝐹(𝐿𝑎 )) reconstructs the ribbon category and its full rational Ward output,
with module tensor product Δ(1)(𝑉 ⊗ 𝑊), where Δ(1) projects onto matching intermediate labels.
Its canonical finite pairing tensor satisfies both coproduct pairing laws and the categorical exchange
identity; its scalar counit need not be multiplicative.
New assembly of known results · Antecedents: Li (1994, preprint); Huang (1995, 2005, preprints); Hayashi (1999,
preprint); Pfeiffer (2007, preprint); Coquereaux–Trinchero (2004).

Abelian bulk sources give full boundary Fock modules
On 𝑀 = ℂ𝑧 × ℝ≥0 , oriented by 𝑑𝑥 ∧ 𝑑𝑦 ∧ 𝑑𝑡, fix 𝜅 ∈ ℂ× and let 𝐴(𝑈) be compactly supported dif‐
ferential forms whose boundary pullback lies in 𝑑𝑧 ∧ Ω∙𝑐 (𝑈 ∩ 𝜕𝑀). The Gaussian observables
⨁𝑚 ≥ 0 Sym𝑚𝑏 ⁡(𝐴(𝑈)[2]) use completed projective bornological tensors at each field number, an al‐
gebraic sum over field numbers, and the BV pairing −𝜅(2𝜋𝑖)−1 ∫𝑈 𝑎 ∧ 𝑏. For a proper vertical ray 𝐿
beginning at the boundary and charge 𝛼 ∈ ℂ, the supported source differential is 𝑑 + Δ𝜅 − 𝑖𝛼𝐼𝐿 ,
where 𝐼𝐿 (𝑎) = ∫𝐿 𝑎 on spatial one-forms and is zero on other linear degrees; its rotation-finite end‐
point cohomology is concentrated in degree zero and equals ℱ𝛼 = ℂ[𝐽−1 , 𝐽−2 , …]|𝛼⟩. The complete
current action is 𝐽−𝑛 = 𝑥𝑛 , 𝐽𝑛 = 𝜅𝑛𝜕𝑥𝑛 for 𝑛 > 0, and 𝐽0 = 𝛼, on algebraic polynomial states.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

A strict dg bi-Galois object for exterior uncurving marks
Let 𝑘 be a characteristic-zero field, let 𝑟 ≥ 0 be finite, and let 𝐻 = Λ(𝜃1 , …, 𝜃𝑟 ) have primitive de‐

gree-one generators and zero differential. Work with algebraic, possibly unbounded dg modules and
direct-sum tensor totalization. If 𝑁𝑖 is the action of 𝜃𝑖 , an uncurving mark 𝜆 ∈ 𝑘𝑟 replaces a module
differential 𝐷 by 𝐷 − ∑𝑖 𝜆𝑖 𝑁𝑖 ; let 𝑊𝜆 be the maps that become quasi-isomorphisms after this re‐
placement. For 𝐾 = Λ(𝜉1 , …, 𝜉𝑟 ), with primitive generators of degree −1 and zero differential, and
𝑎 ∈ 𝑘𝑟 ,

𝐿𝑎 = (Λ(𝜂1 , …, 𝜂𝑟 ), 𝑑𝜂𝑖 = 𝑎𝑖 ),

|𝜂𝑖 | = −1,

with the primitive left and right translation coactions of 𝐾, is a strict dg bi-Galois object. Its coin‐
variants are 𝑘, both canonical maps are dg isomorphisms, and algebraic cotensor by 𝐿𝑎 sends
(𝑀, 𝐷) to (𝑀, 𝐷 − ∑𝑖 𝑎𝑖 𝑁𝑖 ). For 𝑎 = 𝜆 − 𝜇 this gives a monoidal equivalence of the localizations
defined by 𝑊𝜆 and 𝑊𝜇 , with 𝐿𝑎 □𝐾 𝐿𝑏 ≅ 𝐿𝑎 + 𝑏 , unit 𝐿0 , and inverse 𝐿−𝑎 . If 𝑎 ≠ 0, the underlying
complex of 𝐿𝑎 is acyclic, so this statement uses strict cotensor and the specified marked
localizations.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Reconstruction from an admissible geometric vacuum
Let 𝑋 be a smooth algebraic curve over ℂ. Suppose a category-valued system, varying under arbi‐
trary parameter pullback, 𝒞𝐼 on every 𝑋 𝐼 , including 𝒞⌀ = Vectℂ ⁡, has coherent pullback functors

for all finite-set maps, equivalences for surjections, unit insertions for injections, and symmetric ex‐
ternal fusion over disjoint configurations, satisfying composition, iterated-partition and mixed
base-change identities. Let its vacua 𝑣𝐼 come from the empty unit, and let 𝐹𝐼 : 𝒞𝐼 → QCoh⁡(𝑋 𝐼 ) be
compatible 𝒪-linear realizations whose vacuum comparisons for surjections and disjoint fusion are
invertible. Require 𝐵𝐼 = 𝐹𝐼 (𝑣𝐼 ) to have no nonzero local sections supported on collision diagonals
and 𝐵⌀ ≅ ℂ as an algebra with its actual unit. Then 𝐵𝐼 is a factorization algebra, and its canonical
connection and residue operation reconstruct a unital chiral algebra on the right differential mod‐
ule 𝜔𝑋 ⊗ 𝐵{1} , with all operadic substitutions. Equivalences preserving these
realized vacuum diagrams give isomorphic unital chiral algebras. On 𝔸1 , translation-equivariant in‐
put gives the corresponding vertex algebra; an explicitly retained conformal vector and grading are
preserved. This is the Beilinson–Drinfeld reconstruction applied to the specified geometric
coefficients.
Known · Antecedents: Beilinson–Drinfeld (2004), Lemma 3.4.5, Proposition 3.4.7, formula (3.4.8.3), Theorems 3.4.9
and 3.5.10, §3.5.11 and Lemma 3.5.12; Raskin, Chiral Categories (2019 manuscript), §§4.24, 6.2–6.3.

W H AT W O U L D C H A N G E T H E P R O G R A M M E

Constructions that would change the programme
The most consequential gaps are maps between independently defined theories: from coefficient duals to
singular fields and modules, from boundary operations to physical bulk observables, and from scalar
traces to prepared states. The questions below give concrete tests for those maps and for major advances
in the individual families. A successful test settles the component stated; the full target still requires its
other operations, domains and coherences.

QUESTION 1

A native Koszul interface with collisions
OPEN PROBLEM

Realize the coefficient dual through native endpoint operations
Construct a filtered realization of the transverse relative bar or module-Hom of independently de‐
fined endpoint modules, and compare its continuous dual with the polynomial line algebra. The
comparison must preserve the positive filtration, actual module-linear mapping complexes, numeri‐
cal and Jordan charge sectors, the distinct empty unit and occupied vacuum, both ordered binary
charts, spectral tensorators and the total position connection. It must induce the original field com‐
parison and the independently normalized associator. The first deciding construction is the
regular/localized/supported Taylor square with both derived vacuum specializations; extend it to
the 13|2 branch before asserting a common collision theory.
W H Y I T M AT T E R S

This would connect associative coefficient duality to independently defined fields, endpoints and fusion
on the same domains. It would make the line algebra a description of the native interface rather than only
a one-position algebra. Analytic specialization and physical generation would still require their own
7

comparisons.
R E L AT E D S TAT E M E N T S

12 statements

12

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Two point-kernel theories are already distinguished
For the polynomial Heisenberg point algebra, the ordinary derived kernel is the local-cohomology
coring 𝑅Γ(𝑡) ℂ[𝑡], with image the 𝑡-power-supported sector. A separate product-bar/hybrid homo‐
topy–derived enhancement has the diagonal Koszul coring and retains every algebraic unbounded
charged complex and all module-linear maps. Finite independent point tensors and the specified
polynomial, fixed-field and energy-product bounds are established. These point kernels do not yet
supply actions and balanced unit/counit for the unaugmented geometric transverse monoid.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Separated charge transport has finite higher operations
On fully separated configurations, the reduced exterior model has a strictly unital finite 𝐴∞ auto‐
morphism and inverse, with finite insertions for arbitrary finite commuting charge matrices, includ‐
ing Jordan blocks. The absolute spectral cocycle is interpreted by rational substitutions before a
common Laurent expansion. The formal comparison criterion retains its full normalized universal
Maurer–Cartan hypothesis; it does not supply the missing supported Taylor target or a finite nu‐
merical-charge homotopy for an unspecified comparison.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Corrected algebraic induction is an equivalence at every level
For every finite-dimensional simple complex Lie algebra and every level, including critical level, cor‐
rected induction gives the full unbounded enhanced equivalence after exactly the induction-detect‐
ed class W_F is inverted. Restricted twisted coinduction supplies the inverse, dg adjunction, unit,
counit and triangle identities on the termwise-smooth source with ordinary direct-sum tensors.
W_F is a proper subclass of ordinary line-module quasi-isomorphisms. The first-kind Hom com‐
parison, its contragredient mark, the separate all-energy marked comparison, completions and geo‐
metric fusion are different targets.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Finite-quotient point duality includes critical level
For the general-linear Yang double of rank n≥2 at each finite quantum precision, point duality
holds between termwise R_N-free complexes of smooth modules, with R_N=C[hbar]/(hbar^N),
N≥1, and graded-projective curved contramodules, using reduction-defined null classes. Support
estimates, finite base change, adjunction and unit/counit are retained, including critical level. The
formal limit, ordinary-derived equivalence and factorization-module comparison are stronger state‐
ments and remain separate.

8

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Induction preserves the marked comparison in every energy
For every finite-dimensional simple complex Lie algebra, every level and every finite horizontal top,
the actual contragredient object and its normalized marked arrow are identified in every energy. At
raw level zero the vacuum arrow has rank one, and its entire cone is computed. The full induction
null class is the explicit thick, direct-sum-closed class generated by exact totalizations and ghost-zero
inflated acyclic smooth current modules. The original unbounded equivalence retains its prescribed
localization; ambient derived-Hom and completion comparisons are separate.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Native ghost coefficients have a compatible dg recipient
For the four-colour general-linear system modulo hbar cubed, the derivative formula holds for every
exterior monomial and jet at every level. Its second-order operator has exterior differential-operator
order at most three, and all generator-left quadratic and cubic ghost rows are explicit. The covariant
Hom recipient has both rooted actions, the correct marked degree and a dg scalar-vacuum retract.
Nonscalar marked evaluation, relative units, native products and supported three-input compar‐
isons still require their own maps.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Full bars retain the selected unary maps and Wilson roofs
The selected binary collision extends to every finite full-bar arity with literal tree and block faces,
source units and the prescribed mixed unary value. Polynomial collision modules have an actual
two-sided-bar comparison. Finite Wilson inputs admit affine roofs under the stated irreducibility
hypotheses; the ADE associator comparison retains its level range and physical-realization condi‐
tion. A product span preserves both algebraic and physical deletion systems when no endpoint-pre‐
serving base map can identify them. Completed sources and larger geometric recipients remain
additional.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

The polarized geometric left vacuum is identified
At nonzero Heisenberg level, the prescribed polarized induced left vacuum is equivalent to the geo‐
metric vacuum module with its left action, ordered cuts, chart overlap, descendants and nonsplit
pole extension. Finite numerical and Jordan right-chart modules have an explicit nilpotent-form
gauge roof. Wick-coordinate coefficient completion preserves the coefficient action and localization
attachment, while ordinary vacuum projection is discontinuous in that topology. The universal allcharge balanced bimodule and common all-strata completion remain distinct targets.

9

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Completed induction has its own support theorem
The complete induction equivalence uses degreewise products of discrete energy fibres, continuous
module maps and separate degreewise lower energy bounds and, for every ε>0, a bound e≥L_ε−εq²
for q≥0, with no negative-tail growth bound. Its actual unit and counit are continuous vector-com‐
plex deformation equivalences. Ordinary quasi-isomorphisms lie in its detected weak class, strictly
at every simple critical level; equality holds in the specified energy-preserving and termwise bound‐
ed-above cases. This differs from the original algebraic induction theorem, whose detected class is
strictly smaller than ordinary quasi-isomorphisms. The separate first-kind roof remains conditional
on its native inputs, with a common termwise lower energy bound and finite cohomology fibres.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Resolved native cells admit marked evaluation

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Actual residual roofs still admit tame refinements
In the original nonzero-level Heisenberg category, actual two-layer roofs can have nonzero secondlayer cohomology and fail the positive-mode Fock hypothesis. Both constructed examples have in‐
coming tame refinements. For a mixed arrow from a tame source into G0, the class whose structure
map kills both original P copies, the raw pullback is tame exactly when its full relation-defect mod‐
ule is acyclic. A second natural construction handles every arrow from a Fock-action source into

tame inclusion remain separate.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Binary action models retain the full pointing
Coherent connected coalgebra maps with unbounded coefficients have finite-arity totalization
models and a global inverse-limit mapping space; coherent word cylinders also retain paths and
pointings. Genuine-vacuum right and left cut constructions give relative primitives and uniform
winding nullity. A separate all-arity pointing has a nonzero insertion defect even on the projected
image, so a prescribed disjoint insertion need not extend. That example does not provide a coun‐
terexample to full action recognition, whose general relative fillers remain to be constructed.

Algebraic lifting with a boundary colour

Over a characteristic-zero field, let 𝑍 = ⨁𝑒 ≥ 0 𝑍𝑒 retract onto 𝐴0 = 𝑍0 by energy-preserving
𝑖, 𝑝, 𝐻, with 𝐻|𝐴 = 0 and 𝑑𝐻 + 𝐻𝑑 = 1 − 𝑖𝑝, and let 𝑉 be any cochain complex. Allow only bulk
0

inputs for a bulk output, using energy-additive operations on 𝑍; allow arbitrary bulk and boundary
inputs for a boundary output, using full algebraic graded Hom; set every boundary-input, bulkoutput profile to zero. Restriction from (𝑍, 𝑉) to (𝐴0 , 𝑉) is an aritywise and profilewise surjective
quasi-isomorphism. An independently specified action of a cofibrant coloured operad with these
profiles therefore lifts, uniquely up to homotopy over the entire fixed bulk, mixed and boundary ac‐
tion; preserving marked operations requires a relative cofibration. The inclusion (𝑖, 1𝑉 ) is strict,
while the projection need not be strict on boundary-output operations.
New assembly of known results · Antecedents: Berger–Moerdijk, Resolution of coloured operads and rectification of
homotopy algebras (2007), Theorems 3.5 and 4.1, Example 1.5.1; Casacuberta–Gutiérrez–Moerdijk–Vogt (2010),
Proposition 1.2 and Definition 2.1.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Transfer retains total weight zero

Over a characteristic-zero field, let 𝑍 = ⨁𝑒 ≥ 0 𝑍𝑒 have an energy-preserving retraction (𝑖, 𝑝, 𝐻)
onto 𝐴0 = 𝑍0 , with 𝐻|𝐴 = 0 and 𝑑𝐻 + 𝐻𝑑 = 1 − 𝑖𝑝. Let 𝒪 be an integer-weighted dg operad
0

whose composition, differential, identity and augmentation 𝒪 → ℐ preserve weight, with ℐ in
weight zero, acting so that weight 𝑤 shifts energy by 𝑤. In the algebraic conilpotent
reduced finite-tree bar 𝐵𝒪, including unary and permitted nullary vertices, the sum 𝐼≠0 of nonzero
total weights is a dg coideal. Transfer gives a twisting morphism 𝐵𝒪/𝐼≠0 → End𝐴0 ⁡ and a cobar ac‐

tion, with an infinity quasi-isomorphism to 𝑍 after pullback to Ω𝐵𝒪. The quotient coproduct
projects each factor to weight zero; it is not a claim that the weight-zero summand is a subcooperad.
A strict unit and multiplication do not automatically admit the required augmentation.

Reductive Poisson formality and the fixed affine action

Let 𝔤 = 𝔷 ⊕︎𝔰 be a finite-dimensional reductive Lie algebra over a characteristic-zero field 𝑘, and let
𝛽 be a nondegenerate invariant symmetric form. The inclusion
((Λ𝔤∗ )𝔤 , 0) ↪ (𝐶 ∗ (𝔤; 𝑘), 𝑑CE )
is a strict dg Poisson quasi-isomorphism for the exterior product and the degree-minus-two inverse𝛽 bracket. On Λ𝔷∗ ⊗ (Λ𝔰∗ )𝔰 , the bracket is the inverse central form and vanishes identically on the
semisimple invariant factor; this invariant-cochain vanishing is a known result. For the universal
polynomial affine complex with 𝛽 = 𝜅 + Kill⁡/2, the same formality holds for an existing
energy-additive action of a fixed cofibrant operad 𝑄 whose restriction factors through a fixed
𝑄 → Pois3 and this exact strict Poisson structure. It does not specify arbitrary additional opera‐
tions or a boundary action, and for a nonzero centre the inverse central form remains part of the
Poisson algebra.
New assembly of known results · Antecedents: Pinczon–Ushirobira (2007), Proposition 8.3 and the normalization in
Proposition 5.4; Koszul (1950), Lemma 9.1 and Theorem 9.2; Chevalley–Eilenberg (1948), §19, equation (19.2) and
Theorem 19.1.

The prescribed Heisenberg binary comparison is strict on all states
Over a characteristic-zero field at nonzero rank-one Heisenberg level 𝜅, use the universal polynomi‐
al complex 𝑍 = 𝑘[𝑢𝑖 : 𝑖 ≥ 0] ⊗ Λ(𝑐𝑖 : 𝑖 ≥ 0), with undivided generators 𝑢𝑖 = 𝑇 𝑖 𝐽, 𝑐𝑖 = 𝑇 𝑖 𝑐, and
𝑑𝑢𝑖 = 𝜅𝑐𝑖 + 1 . For the two-copy Heisenberg comparison, its comparison homotopy
ℎ = ℎpath − ℎcone , projection 𝑃, and independently defined geometric roof pairing, the comparison

𝐺𝜆 (𝑎, 𝑏) = 𝑃[ℎ(𝑎)𝜆 ℎ(𝑏)] is zero for every pair of polynomial states. The corresponding pairings

and specified secondary binary brackets agree strictly. This identifies the prescribed binary compari‐
son; it does not identify an independently constructed higher geometric action.

The nonabelian binary comparison through energy two
For split 𝔰𝔩2 over ℚ[𝑠, 𝑠 −1 ], with [𝐸, 𝐹] = 𝐻, (𝐸, 𝐹) = 1, (𝐻, 𝐻) = 2, raw level 𝑠 − 2 and shifted

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

The two-copy affine field comparison on all algebraic states
For a finite-dimensional complex Lie algebra and universal affine vacua at invariant forms 𝜅 and
−𝜅 − Kill⁡, the specified projected two-copy fields agree on every algebraic state. The theorem re‐
tains the absolute ghosts, algebraic energy sum, full regular coefficients, and prescribed contractions
and projection. The primary equality requires no inverse form. When 𝛽 = 𝜅 + Kill⁡/2 is invertible,
the secondary on all algebraic ghost jets is the average of the two endpoint extractions of the levelzero vertex-Poisson bracket. Some current-containing inputs fail the distinct residue comparison.
Native supported operations and an independent geometric bulk action remain additional
constructions.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Horizontal coherence, regular modules and a cobar inverse

For finite-dimensional complex 𝔤 and any invariant symmetric form, the fixed-contraction rational
gauge gives five literal four-position edges and a relative pentagon filler in their common recipient.
Its nested expansions complete in displacements before localizing, and its choice of contraction and
parameter section remains fixed. A translation-equivariant bar contraction also lifts a specified ten‐
sor-bar intertwiner horizontally, retaining finite support, whole-output bounds, and continuity on
each fixed-denominator module. Fixed Tamari pulling triangulations carry strict units and coherent
choices at every finite arity. Full bars admit evaluation-order families on permutohedra with literal
nested block facets, source-unit restrictions, and interchange paths. Strict disjoint interchange is
false, including on some individual pure-colour full-bar words; this does not invalidate the four-po‐
sition relative pentagon. Unital comparison on finite PL subdivisions is also available for the same
pointwise deletion maps, preserving endpoint families while allowing refinement. Ordered-cut-nor‐
malized mixed unary lifts and a relative gauge-comparison square also exist. Arbitrary prescribed
unary boundaries have a stated relative obstruction, whose vanishing is not automatic. The faithful
regular right module 𝐶 ⊗ 𝑁 realizes the tensor and full-bar gauges with their parameter cells, units
and collapse maps. An explicit algebraic cobar map 𝐹 : Ω𝑋 → Ω𝐶 retracts Ωsh exactly; its linear
term is Alexander–Whitney and its output length on 𝑥 is at most 𝜙(𝑥) + 1. The other composite
has a polynomial dg-algebra homotopy to the identity relative to Ωsh. This gives the finite algebraic
DCSH inverse, while the strict coalgebra inverse remains excluded. Changing the deletion maps,
unrestricted completed duality and the comparison with actual native vacuum or endpoint opera‐
tions remain separate. That comparison must retain the native coproduct, retraction, binary
tensorator, connection and every unit and facet; fixed-factor-frame obstructions do not exclude
changing endpoint frames.

QUESTION 2

Positive type of the two-boundary kernel
OPEN PROBLEM

Which charges give a positive kernel?

For a closed oriented surface of genus 𝑔 ≥ 2, let 𝒯𝑔 be its smooth marked Teichmüller space, with
hyperbolic curvature −1, and choose a base point 𝑜. Let 𝑆 be the classical Liouville action on the
quasi-Fuchsian product, 𝐷(𝑋, 𝑍) = 8𝜋(2𝑔 − 2) − 𝑆(𝑋, 𝑍), and let 𝐹𝑜 be the base-point-normal‐
ized Hermitian holomorphic polarization of Φ𝑜 (𝑋) = 𝐷(𝑋, 𝑜), so that 𝐹𝑜 (𝑋, 𝑋) = Φ𝑜 (𝑋) and
𝐹𝑜 (𝑋, 𝑜) = 0. Determine

𝒫𝑔 = { 𝑐 ∈ [0, ∞) : 𝐾𝑐 (𝑋, 𝑍) = exp⁡( 𝑐𝐹𝑜24(𝑋𝜋, 𝑍) ) is of positive type on 𝒯𝑔 };

positive type requires every finite matrix (𝐾𝑐 (𝑋𝑖 , 𝑋𝑗 )) to be positive semidefinite. The problem re‐
mains open at each fixed genus, including the positive unitary minimal charges and 𝑐 ≥ 1.
W H Y I T M AT T E R S

Positive type turns the polarized action into a Hilbert-space pairing of holomorphic evaluation vectors. It
is a global restriction on all finite configurations of complex structures, stronger than positivity of the di‐
agonal metric or of a fixed collection of derivatives.
R E L AT E D S TAT E M E N T S

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Nonminimal charges fail near large embedded discs

Fix a real charge 0 < 𝑐 < 1 outside {1 − 6/[𝑝(𝑝 + 1)] : 𝑝 ∈ ℤ, 𝑝 ≥ 2}. There is a finite radius 𝑟∗ (𝑐)
such that, on any closed curvature-−1 hyperbolic surface of genus 𝑔 ≥ 2 with a point of
injectivity radius at least 𝑟∗ (𝑐), the normalized Liouville kernel 𝐾𝑐 = exp⁡(𝑐𝐹𝑜 /(24𝜋)) has a nega‐
tive finite derivative form and negative finite point matrices arbitrarily near that surface in 𝒯𝑔 .
Consequently 𝑐 ∉ 𝒫𝑔 for every sufficiently large integer 𝑔. At 𝑐 = 1/4, radius 2400 suffices: a com‐

pactly supported Beltrami family gives nine actual Teichmüller points and a quadratic form at most
−837/1024. These are exclusions for the closed-surface kernel; the negative Virasoro vacuum forms
used as input are known.
New · Antecedents: Friedan–Qiu–Shenker, Details of the non-unitarity proof for highest weight representations of the
Virasoro algebra (1986), Theorem 1; Neretin (2016), Corollary 1.3 and equation (3.4); Teo (2005), Theorems 3.5 and 4.3.
The large-disc surfaces use the classical polygon geometry described by Stepanyants–Beardon–Paton–Krioukov (2024), §V,
equations (18)–(21).

Every two-point matrix is positive

For the normalized kernel 𝐾𝑐 = exp⁡(𝑐𝐹𝑜 /(24𝜋)) on smooth marked 𝒯𝑔 , 𝑔 ≥ 2, every two-point

matrix is positive semidefinite for 𝑐 ≥ 0, and positive definite for 𝑐 > 0 and distinct 𝑋, 𝑍. For 𝑐 > 0
and 𝑋 ≠ 𝑍, with 𝜈 = 𝑐/(24𝜋) and Liouville diastasis 𝐷, its determinant is

𝐾𝑐 (𝑋, 𝑋)𝐾𝑐 (𝑍, 𝑍)(1 − 𝑒−𝜈𝐷(𝑋, 𝑍) ) > 0.
Teo’s identity 𝐷 = 12𝜋𝑆2 gives 𝐷 ≥ 0, with equality only when 𝑋 = 𝑍. This two-point inequality
leaves larger matrices unrestricted.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

A positive realization would have infinite rank

For every 𝑔 ≥ 2 and real 𝑐 > 0, the normalized Liouville kernel 𝐾𝑐 = exp⁡(𝑐𝐹𝑜 /(24𝜋)) on all of 𝒯𝑔

cannot simultaneously have positive type and finite rank. Thus any positive Hilbert realization
must be infinite-dimensional. The obstruction uses the inverse-cubic Weil–Petersson metric along a
one-node logarithmic plumbing collar and projective unitary monodromy; the rank-one constant
kernel at 𝑐 = 0 is allowed.
New assembly of known results · Antecedents: Wolpert, Geometry of the Weil–Petersson completion of Teichmüller space
(2005, preprint), Theorem 2(2), Corollary 4 and §5; Calabi rigidity as presented by Loi–Zedda (2017, preprint), Theorem
2.2.5.

The positivity set is a closed additive semigroup

For each fixed 𝑔 ≥ 2, the set 𝒫𝑔 = {𝑐 ≥ 0 : exp⁡(𝑐𝐹𝑜 /(24𝜋)) is of positive type on 𝒯𝑔 } is closed in

[0, ∞), contains 0, and satisfies 𝒫𝑔 + 𝒫𝑔 ⊆ 𝒫𝑔 . The identities 𝐾0 = 1 and 𝐾𝑐 + 𝑑 = 𝐾𝑐 𝐾𝑑 , together
with the Schur product theorem, give these properties; they do not produce a positive charge.

Every fixed jet order is eventually positive

For each fixed point 𝑋 ∈ 𝒯𝑔 , 𝑔 ≥ 2, and each fixed integer 𝑚 ≥ 0, the full degree-at-most-𝑚 coeffi‐
cient matrix of 𝐾𝑐 = exp⁡(𝑐𝐹𝑜 /(24𝜋)) at 𝑋 is positive definite for all sufficiently large real 𝑐. The
threshold may depend on both 𝑋 and 𝑚. The same assertion holds locally for the exponential of the
polarization of any real-analytic strictly plurisubharmonic potential; it gives no single charge that
works for all orders.
New assembly of known results · Antecedents: The leading Fock coefficients and stability of finite positive matrices; the allorder framework is Loi–Zedda (2017, preprint), Theorems 2.2.4 and 2.2.6.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

The regularized mixed Grunsky identity

Fix a base surface 𝑜 ∈ 𝒯𝑔 , 𝑔 ≥ 2, uniformized by a torsion-free cocompact group Γ, and the fixed

conformal end of its Bers slice. On the Bergman Hilbert space 𝐻 = 𝐴 2 (𝔻) of holomorphic oneforms, the actual Grunsky operators 𝐵𝑋 : 𝐻 → 𝐻 form a norm-holomorphic Γ-equivariant family
with 𝐵𝑜 = 0 and ‖𝐵𝑋 ‖ < 1. With the fundamental-domain trace 𝜏Γ normalized by 𝜏Γ (1) = 𝑔 − 1,
the base-point-normalized Liouville kernel satisfies

The trace belongs to the equivariant operator algebra

For a torsion-free cocompact surface group Γ of genus 𝑔 ≥ 2 acting on the Bergman space
𝐻 = 𝐴 2 (𝔻) of holomorphic one-forms, integration of the diagonal operator kernel over a funda‐

trast, a nonzero actual closed-surface Grunsky product 𝐵𝐵 ∗ on the full disc Bergman space has infi‐
nite ordinary trace.

For a finite-dimensional nonabelian Lie algebra over a characteristic-zero field, with invariant sym‐

A continuous native-product bar comparison at one position

Let 𝔤 be a finite-dimensional complex reductive Lie algebra, 𝜅 an invariant symmetric form, and
𝛽 = 𝜅 + Kill⁡/2 nondegenerate. At one position, use the universal polynomial affine Chevalley–
Eilenberg complex 𝑍, with positive PBW weight 2𝑛 + 1 on the current 𝑗𝑛 = 𝑥−𝑛 − 1 and ghost 𝑐𝑛 ,

and the completion of its reduced tensor bar by that weight. Starting from the opposite regular as‐
sociative PBW product, there is a coalgebra change of coordinates continuous in both directions
that gives a strictly unital augmented 𝐴∞ structure whose binary operation is exactly the native nor‐
mally ordered product. Its inverse Taylor terms vanish above arity 4𝑒 at positive energy 𝑒, and above
arity one at energy zero. The continuous finite-support graded dual of this completed bar is quasiisomorphic to the polynomial line algebra 𝑌𝛽 , with no completion in zero-mode length. The full
translation and Sugawara operators extend to continuous higher bar coderivations and are inter‐
twined with the line operators. This one-position comparison does not identify the prescribed
product homotopy or supply geometric collisions and an induced-module functor.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Ordinary derived cochains lose Wilson tensor products

Let 𝑘 have characteristic zero, let 𝔤 = 𝔰𝔩2 (𝑘), and let 𝑅 be its defining two-dimensional representa‐
tion. The ordinary Chevalley–Eilenberg complex 𝐶 ∗ (𝔤; 𝑅) is acyclic, whereas 𝐶 ∗ (𝔤; 𝑅 ⊗ 𝑅) has a
copy of 𝑘 in degrees zero and three. Therefore the functor to ordinary derived 𝐶 ∗ (𝔤; 𝑘)-modules
cannot be a faithful strong monoidal realization of these Wilson charges: the derived tensor of the
two zero images is zero, while the coefficient tensor has nonzero cohomology.
Known · Antecedents: Chevalley–Eilenberg (1948), Theorem 24.1, pp. 117–119.

Finite rooted forests and a necessary marked quotient
On the admitted independent external-product domains, relative multiplication gives every finite
rooted forest with all contact terms, holomorphic normal action, root-compatible nested cuts,
transported relabelling and both occupied-vacuum formulas. Nonroot landings, dependent nor‐
mals and height-wall crossings need additional maps. For the specified algebraic finite-normal-order

forces two principal-part copies. This excludes the stated nonnegative finite-stage sheet quotients
with tangentially injective degree-one modules. It does not exclude all native intermediate recipients
or prove that the necessary torsion classes suffice to construct the lift.

What the columns mean

Algebra and geometry

Koszul dual

The field algebra or geometric coefficient object, with its multiplication, colli‐
sion maps, exchange and vacuum.

The dual construction, its curvature and completion, the category in which it
lives, and any biduality or complementary-parameter statement.

Module comparison

Fusion and exchange

A functor or equivalence between explicitly defined representation categories,
including their weak equivalences and distinguished vacuum objects.

Products of representations at moving points, associators, braiding, and recon‐
struction from a fibre functor.

Bulk operations

Lines and endpoints

Local bulk observables or a candidate algebra of vacuum endomorphisms, to‐
gether with higher operations and their boundary action.

Line defects, their endpoint modules and couplings, and flat connections gov‐
erning their motion.

States and sewing

Physical comparison

State spaces and preparation by amplitudes, with the analytic or formal do‐
mains needed for gluing.

Source and Ward identities, parameter normalization, and comparisons with a
bulk theory defined independently of the boundary algebra.

A free field with two independent parameters

Over ℂ, let 𝜅, 𝜆 ∈ ℂ and give 𝑉 = ℂ[𝑥1 , 𝑥2 , …], 𝑇𝑥𝑟 = 𝑟𝑥𝑟 + 1 , the Wick contraction
𝑃(𝑢) = 𝜅𝑢−2 + 𝜆𝑢−1 . Its exchange satisfies 𝑆(𝑢)(𝑥1 ⊗ 𝑥1 ) = 𝑥1 ⊗ 𝑥1 − 2𝜆𝑢−1 1 ⊗ 1, and its or‐
dinary unital geometric point modules with all marked collisions are exactly the
smooth modules with [𝐽𝑚 , 𝐽𝑛 ] = (𝜅𝑚𝛿𝑚 + 𝑛, 0 + 𝜆𝜀(𝑚)𝛿𝑚 + 𝑛, −1 )1, where 𝜀(𝑚) = 1 for 𝑚 ≥ 0
and −1 otherwise. The curved dual has zero differential and curvature
∑𝑟 ≥ 1 (𝜅𝑟𝑒𝑟 + 𝜆𝑒𝑟 − 1 )𝑒−𝑟 on exterior cochains completed by finite negative-mode cutoffs sep‐
arately in each exterior degree, with finite positive-mode support at each cutoff. For any two
contractions of this form, the primitive field map 𝑉𝑃 + 𝑄 → 𝑉𝑃 ⊗ 𝑉𝑄 preserves fields and ex‐
change and adds the contraction parameters; it is not a fixed-level fusion coproduct.

Fock modules and rational coinvariants

Over ℂ at 𝜅 ≠ 0, consider Heisenberg modules with [𝑎𝑚 , 𝑎𝑛 ] = 𝑚𝜅𝛿𝑚 + 𝑛, 0 , each positive
mode locally nilpotent, and sufficiently high positive modes annihilating each vector. The as‐
signment 𝐸 ↦ ℱ(𝐸) = 𝐸[𝑥1 , 𝑥2 , …], with 𝑎−𝑚 = 𝑥𝑚 , 𝑎𝑚 = 𝑚𝜅𝜕𝑥𝑚 and 𝑎0 = 𝐴𝐸 , is an exact
equivalence from ℂ[𝑎0 ]-modules, and negative-current coinvariants recover 𝐸. At finitely
many distinct complex points, the derived coinvariants of the algebraic tensor product of these
Fock modules under rational currents vanishing at infinity are ⨂𝑖 𝐸𝑖 in degree zero. For finitedimensional 𝐸𝑖 , their coinvariant connection is 𝑑 + 𝜅−1 ∑𝑖 < 𝑗 𝐴𝑖 𝐴𝑗 𝑑 log⁡(𝑝𝑖 − 𝑝𝑗 ); on scalarcharge dual fibres the positive half-exchange is 𝑒 𝜋𝑖𝛼𝛽/𝜅 followed by the flip, with normalized as‐
sociator equal to one.

Grading changes the derived endomorphisms

For the algebraic Heisenberg mode algebra 𝐴𝜅 over ℂ, with 𝜅 ≠ 0, put 𝔫− = ⨁𝑚 ≥ 1 ℂ𝑎−𝑚 ,
𝑊 = 𝐴𝜅 ⊗𝑈(𝔫−) ℂ and 𝑅 = ℂ[𝑎0 ]. With deg⁡𝑎𝑛 = 𝑛, internally graded derived Hom, summed
over internal degrees but retaining full products within each homogeneous component, gives
a formal endomorphism dg algebra with value 𝑅. In the ordinary unbounded derived category
of all algebraic 𝐴𝜅 -modules, Ext0 ⁡(𝑊, 𝑊) = 𝑅, Ext1 ⁡(𝑊, 𝑊) = ( ∏𝑚 ≥ 1 𝑉𝑚 )/( ⨁𝑚 ≥ 1 𝑉𝑚 ),
where 𝑉𝑚 = 𝑎𝑚 𝑅[𝑎𝑚 , 𝑎𝑚 + 1 , …], and every other degree vanishes. For every
polynomial Fock module ℱ(𝐸), full RHom⁡(𝑊, ℱ(𝐸)) vanishes, whereas derived negativecurrent coinvariants equal 𝐸[0].

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

The Gaussian bulk and its boundary current

On ℂ × ℝ≥0 , take the Gaussian abelian Chern–Simons observable complex over ℂ, with nu‐
merical 𝑘 = ℏ𝜅 ≠ 0, pairing −𝜅(2𝜋𝑖)−1 ∫ 𝛼 ∧ 𝛽, and boundary condition 𝑗∗ 𝛼 ∈ 𝑑𝑧 ∧ Ω∙𝑐 . Its
carrier is the direct sum over field number of completed projective bornological symmetric
powers, realized by compact smooth kernels satisfying that boundary condition in every vari‐
able. The rotation-finite boundary cohomology is the universal rank-one Heisenberg vertex al‐
gebra 𝑉𝑘 , while interior cohomology is an exterior algebra on one degree-one class. With ori‐
entation 𝑑𝑥 ∧ 𝑑𝑦 ∧ 𝑑𝑡, the outward-oriented sphere operation and the generator 𝜉 normal‐
ized by 𝜄𝐽0 𝜉 = 1, the bulk bracket is {𝜉, 𝜉} = 2𝜋𝑖/𝑘, and the corresponding finite-jet vacuum
cohomology has algebraic secondary pairing 1/𝑘.

Costello–Francis–Gwilliam (2026).

Vacuum extensions detect the Heisenberg level

Over ℂ at 𝜅 ≠ 0, restrict the polynomial Heisenberg Fock construction to finite-dimensional
ℂ[𝑡]-modules with nilpotent 𝑡, where 𝑎0 = 𝑡, and transport the product by
ℱ(𝑀) ⋆ ℱ(𝑁) = ℱ(𝑀 ⊗ 𝑁). Prescribe the conformal-block connection
𝑑 − (𝑇𝑀 ⊗ 𝑇𝑁 /𝜅) 𝑑𝑧/𝑧 on these chosen coefficient spaces, with the ordinary associator; the
positive half-exchange is flip⁡exp⁡(𝜋𝑖𝑇𝑀 ⊗ 𝑇𝑁 /𝜅). On the marked vacuum extension
𝐽 = ℂ[𝑡]/(𝑡 2 ), with its inclusion and quotient maps 𝑖 : ℂ → 𝐽, 𝑝 : 𝐽 → ℂ, normalized by
𝑖(1) = 𝑡, 𝑝(1) = 1, 𝑝(𝑡) = 0, and 𝑛 = 𝑖𝑝, positive pure braid is 1 + (2𝜋𝑖/𝜅)𝑛 ⊗ 𝑛. A complexlinear exact strong braided equivalence preserving this exact marked extension and its unit
identification between levels 𝜅, 𝜅′ ≠ 0 therefore requires 𝜅 = 𝜅′ , although the ambient associa‐
tive algebra RHomℂ[𝑡] ⁡(ℂ, ℂ) ≃ Λ(𝜖), |𝜖| = 1, is unchanged.
New assembly of known results · Antecedents: Milas (2006); Etingof–Gelaki (2007); Runkel (2012); Allen–
Lentner–Schweigert–Wood (2025).

OPEN PROBLEM

A comparison preserving the vacuum and its operations

For nonzero-level Heisenberg modules over ℂ, construct an independently defined compari‐
son between negative-current coinvariants and the degreewise completed curved cochain
transform on one common localized category, preserving the marked vacuum, translation, ra‐
tional collision maps and logarithmic transport. Extend the Gaussian endpoint construction
to nilpotent vacuum extensions with compatible transport and framing: for two unit charges
at level three, increasing-normal-coordinate graph transport around a positive full turn gives
𝑒−2𝜋𝑖/3 , whereas positive Fock continuation gives 𝑒2𝜋𝑖/3 . An opposite-transport functor or a
comparison with the reverse braided target remains to be constructed, together with the com‐
parison preserving mixed operations of independent local bulk observables. The outgoing col‐
lision normalization is taken on the positive-real branch in this phase test.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

A coefficient-native category of charged endpoints
For the Gaussian charged-endpoint construction, a chosen propagator defines a bounded dg
product 𝐵𝑃 on the compact smooth native carrier. The finite-free singleton coefficient catego‐
ry has all bounded 𝐵𝑃 -linear maps, strict composition and units, balanced tensor and finite
charged duals satisfying both triangles. Collar and moment functors are quasi-equivalences at
this singleton scope. A specified continuous product bar contracts by a conjugated Euler ho‐
motopy, and smooth position families carry the actual differential-form correction. Separately,
ordinary tensor descends in the split module-homotopy category, the polynomial tower has
all-arity units on every dg 𝐸-module, and polynomial Wick maps have transported-operator
corrections on the stated large-separation Laurent domain. These are coefficient and modulehomotopy comparisons: the charged graded-free kernel remains non-K-flat in the ordinary de‐
rived category. Two compact sources of charges 𝑞, −𝑞 in one ball give a concrete failure of the
naive all-position map to one energy-completed multipole point. Other recipients remain pos‐
sible. The later point-derived local-cohomology coring and all-charge hybrid diagonal coring
are specified separately below. The full native cut actor, occupied vacua, common
collision/separation completion, nonsplit geometric left endpoint, invariant geometric bal‐
anced coring and physical sewing remain open.

60

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Point-derived duality and the separate all-charge coring
For the specified exterior-point and polynomial oscillator coefficient algebras, the genuine or‐
dinary derived adjunction has kernel 𝑅Γ(𝑡) 𝐵, 𝐵 = 𝑘[𝑡], with an explicit Čech counit, coprod‐
uct, idempotence and triangle identities. Its image is the 𝑡-power-supported derived sector. A
separate hybrid coefficient enhancement admits every algebraic unbounded charged complex
and all graded module-linear maps, with the diagonal Koszul coring and balanced triangles.
The charged graded-free kernel remains non-K-flat over the exterior algebra; its ordinary and
derived tensors are different. The full oscillator algebra has an actual derived unit roof. The
product-bar homotopy has total-count bounds on the stated polynomial, fixed-field native
and oscillator-energy domains; an arbitrary completion mixing infinitely many counts in one
coefficient needs a further estimate. Finite independent point tensors retain these construc‐
tions. Collision-chart gluing, the geometric transverse actor and its balanced coring remain
separate.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Finite separated Wick maps and chosen primitive transport
On the reduced exterior algebra over the fully separated configuration space, the explicit strict‐
ly unital 𝐴∞ automorphism has arity at most the number of positions, a finite inverse, and fi‐
nite insertions for arbitrary commuting charge matrices, including Jordan blocks, with all co‐
efficient morphisms. A prescribed comparison is matched only under the stated relative, nor‐
malized-bar and complete universal formal Maurer–Cartan class hypotheses; this does not
produce every numerical or supported relative homotopy. Absolute spectral identities use ra‐
tional-kernel jet substitutions before expansion into one common Laurent domain. The strict
Wick representative has no continuous extension from the full normal Taylor energy product
to the specified finite-pole target. Other representatives and completions are not excluded. A
chosen interior primitive difference gives bounded conjugations of the coefficient actions, full
morphism composition and finite duals; specified additive choices compose strictly. Boundary
restrictions, arbitrary-choice independence and physical sewing remain open.

Affine Kac–Moody algebras at irrational
shifted level

8 statements

Universal affine algebras have a curved point dual, a supported module comparison and a collision
product on finite Weyl filtrations. The finite-dimensional top category has the KZ associator and
quantum-group braiding; identifying these algebraic constructions with an independently defined
Chern–Simons boundary functor remains open.

KZ fusion with an explicit collision comparison

For a finite-dimensional simple complex 𝔤, long-root square two and 𝜅 = 𝑘 + ℎ∨ ∉ ℚ, induce
𝕍𝑘 (𝐸) from finite-dimensional 𝔤-modules 𝐸, with positive currents acting trivially on the top.
Their fixed-level product is 𝕍𝑘 (𝐸) ⊙ 𝕍𝑘 (𝐹) = 𝕍𝑘 (𝐸 ⊗ 𝐹), with energy completion ∏ℎ 𝑋[ℎ] ,
associator ΦKZ (−Ω12 /𝜅, −Ω23 /𝜅), and upper-half-plane vertex braiding flip⁡ 𝑒 𝜋𝑖Ω/𝜅 , where
Ω is the Casimir tensor of 𝐵. The rational-current Chevalley–Eilenberg collision map from
distinct points to the completed one-point product is a quasi-isomorphism on modules with a
finite Weyl filtration and finite-dimensional tops. The comparison extends to bounded-above
complexes in that exact category by completing each term before direct-sum totalization; the
completed target need not itself have finite support.

An affine endomorphism obstruction

Over ℂ, use algebraic Laurent-polynomial 𝔰𝔩2 modes at any raw level 𝑘, with [𝑒, 𝑓] = ℎ,
(𝑒, 𝑓) = 1 and (ℎ, ℎ) = 2, and put 𝑊𝑘 = 𝐴𝑘 ⊗𝑈(𝑧−1𝔰𝔩2[𝑧−1]) ℂ. In full algebraic Chevalley–
Eilenberg cochains computing ordinary RHom𝐴𝑘 ⁡(𝑊𝑘 , 𝑊𝑘 ), the degree-one cochain sending
𝑓−1 to the induced vacuum and every other negative basis mode to zero represents a nonzero
class at every 𝑘. Consequently this endomorphism dg algebra cannot be quasi-isomorphic to
𝑈(𝔰𝔩2 )op concentrated in degree zero, including at irrational shifted level. This assertion uses
algebraic modes and full Hom; it does not replace the coinvariant construction of the KZ
connection.

Vacuum composition at every invariant level

Let 𝔤 be a finite-dimensional complex Lie algebra, 𝐵 an invariant symmetric form, and 𝑠 ∈ ℂ.
Put ℓ = −𝑠𝐵 − Kill𝔤 ⁡/2. The affine vacuum cochain algebra 𝐴𝑠 has a filtered multiplicative

The ambient contragredient vacuum and a finite-energy obstruction
With the same affine data, take the full algebraic dual separately in each energy component of
a graded-projective vacuum resolution. Its direct sum over energies is a graded-injective resolu‐
tion of the contragredient vacuum. Signed transposition gives rev𝐸 𝐴𝑠 ≃ 𝑅 Endwt
𝑈ℓ ⁡(𝐷𝑉ℓ ) in or‐

the natural inclusion of lower-bounded modules with finite-dimensional energy spaces into all
graded modules misses the horizontal 𝐻 3 (𝔤; ℂ) self-extension classes. Thus this inclusion is
not derived fully faithful. The full regular module has infinite-dimensional energy-zero space
and requires a different comparison.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Intrinsic vacuum marks and the modular BRST correction
For every finite-dimensional complex Lie algebra, retain smoothness and joint nilpotence of
the positive-current action. Under the specified generic line-action inputs, the intrinsic catego‐
ry has the bounded-below equivalence and smooth full-faithfulness comparison, including the
actual vacuum and augmentation marks, homogeneous endomorphisms and heart kernel.
Intermediate energy spaces may be infinite-dimensional. With 𝛿(𝑥) = tr⁡(ad⁡𝑥), the differen‐
tial 𝑄𝛿 = 𝑄 + 𝑐0 (𝛿) gives the corrected semiregular coreflector for ordinary smooth modules
and bounded-below direct-sum complexes. The unmodified functor carries the negative-char‐
acter twist. The diagonal comparison is the enhanced counit/augmentation comparison on
the intrinsic image. Arbitrary unbounded totals, coefficientwise BRST-to-diagonal maps, the
full regular contragredient and native collision comparisons remain separate.

Noncritical rational fusion and resonance

Let 𝔤 be a finite-dimensional simple complex Lie algebra with long-root square two, 𝑘 ∈ ℚ,
and 𝜅 = 𝑘 + ℎ∨ ≠ 0. On finitely generated smooth universal-affine modules locally finite un‐
der 𝔤[[𝑧]], with their Sugawara grading-restricted generalized module structure, the rationalcurrent fusion product represents the Huang–Lepowsky–Zhang 𝑃(𝑧)-intertwining maps for
𝑧 ≠ 0. For finite-dimensional Weyl tops, its associator and braiding are obtained by reducing
the KZ operators on formal fusion lattices over ℂ[[𝜀]] at 𝜅 + 𝜀; direct evaluation is valid when
constituent conformal weights have no nonzero integral differences in each pairwise and triple
top tensor product. Resonant lattices can be nonstandard, so an unqualified substitution into
a generic KZ matrix is insufficient.

Continuous represented fusion and five-point sewing

For real 𝑐 > 1, extend the four-completed-input five-point collar maps and their natural typed

Completed principal sewing and the geometric tree sector

For every real 𝑐 > 1, compact-uniform three-leg estimates give exponentially weighted input
maps, a nonempty two-successive-insertion domain with strict radius loss, and a local fiveprincipal sewing germ. Internal momentum tails remain uncut. The stated four-point bound‐
ary-input hypothesis is retained where used. At each fixed 𝑐 > 25, a separate geometric theo‐
rem supplies finite-tree local sewing and principal-marked sphere-tree Hilbert/channel com‐
parison on Teichmüller space. It retains the Schwarzian and decoration lines and internal-mo‐
mentum-dependent phases. These results do not construct a category representing all contin‐
uous Ward products or a globally stable external completion.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Five-point collar coherence with four completed inputs

For real 𝑐 > 1, positive principal momenta and the stated positive Shapovalov and four-point
boundary-input hypothesis 𝐻, five independently constructed sewing maps extend to all four
completed inputs on one entire-energy Fréchet domain. They take values in a fixed regularized
output space over the five collar strips. A finite input exponent may depend on a compact po‐
sition set; the sufficient collar widths remain non-effective. The five flip identities hold on their
overlaps, and the independent spectral-label pentagon gives collar coherence. The correspond‐
ing typed positive spectral category has natural bounded spectral morphisms and closed uni‐
tary associators, with inverses on the same output energy scales. Its mode domain has compact
total-output spectrum and finite output level; intermediate spectra need not be compact. Full
integer-mode Ward covariance includes the normally convergent negative-mode sum on
strengthened inputs. External spectral measures remain multiplicities, and fusion retains its
canonical output measure. The generating function has radius at least one; the stated remov‐
able singularity at 𝑐 = 25 still refutes universal exact radius one. Point-label evaluation, colli‐
sion-closed entire inputs, a rational-Ward universal property, all unit triangles and general inte‐
rior or higher-arity sewing remain open.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Four-point comparison on completed descendant inputs

For real central charge in a compact subset of (1, ∞) and positive principal momenta, all-de‐
scendant middle-leg doubling gives a common completed four-point comparison under the
stated boundary-input hypothesis. All three finite input legs lie in weighted Hilbert spaces
with exponent 𝑡 > 𝑇𝐾 , depending on the position compact. The spectator/output exponent
remains 𝑠 > max⁡(𝐴0 /2, log⁡𝐿/2). The construction is bounded in the prescribed direct-inte‐
gral Hilbert and Bochner 𝐿 1 targets and retains the actual channel branches. Its atomless de‐
composable operator is not globally Hilbert–Schmidt. Arbitrary-completed-input five-point
coherence, a universal collision category, unit triangles and all-arity interior sewing remain ad‐
ditional results.

71

CONDITIONAL

Conditional extremal and Parseval comparisons
For the specified positive translation-periodic partner weight, the holomorphic fibre has an ex‐
tremal orthogonal basis. Complete block reciprocity is equivalent to identifying the blocks
with that basis and their prescribed norms. An actual monomial Gram identity gives isometric
comparison of the polynomial closures. Density in the coefficient space and density in the full
Bergman space are distinct requirements. A one-sided kernel inequality yields equality when
the exact total traces agree. Global modular dimension formulas additionally require full co‐
variance and the fixed cocycle. These criteria do not prove the physical nonlinear Gram identi‐
ty, either density condition, positivity or cusp control; numerical trace agreement does not dis‐
charge an exact identity.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Completed vacuum placements and the resolved nested norm

Under the same real 𝑐 > 1 analytic and boundary-input hypotheses, a rapid completed vacu‐
um module 𝑉𝜈 acts on finite exponential principal-input stages on the stated strict annuli. Its
two placements are 𝑌(𝜔, 𝑧) and 𝑒 𝑧𝐿−1 𝑌(𝜔, −𝑧), with the prescribed translation margins; the
vacuum vector has coefficient one. Actual Ward compatibility extends to completed inputs
with all principal spectra retained. Separately, for fixed positive external labels the resolved
three-input vector retains its intermediate multiplicity label and satisfies ‖𝑉‖2 = 2−1/2 𝑁; the
vector 21/4 𝑉 has norm squared 𝑁. The proof sums the outer vacuum series after the finite
Ward operations and then continues the resulting holomorphic pairing. It does not flatten
both raw vacuum series into one unrestricted absolute double series or compute the norm af‐
ter integrating out multiplicity. Categorical unit triangles and a natural exponential replace‐
ment for 𝑉𝜈 remain separate.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

The reflected Virasoro category and its actual marks
For every complex charge 𝑐, the specified line dg algebra 𝑌𝑐 acts coherently on the resolution
and weight-pro carrier of 𝑊26 − 𝑐 . The actual right-action map preserves differential and oppo‐
site composition. It gives the declared cohomologically bounded-below enhanced equivalence,
including the full subcategory of bounded-below smooth Virasoro complexes of charge
26 − 𝑐 whose cohomology is jointly nilpotent under the positive subalgebra 𝔫2 ; no lower in‐
ternal-energy bound is imposed. The inverse, unit and counit are explicit. The regular module
maps to 𝑊, the universal-vacuum mark to 𝑉26 − 𝑐 , and the augmentation to the restricted con‐
tragredient 𝐷𝜎 𝑉26 − 𝑐 . The internal weight completion and derived bimodule comparison are
essential; this is not a strict full action on the degree-zero module alone. The unbounded
null class, relation to the different charge-𝑐 induction localization, native collision maps and
physical realization remain separate.

Braided quantum Hamiltonian reduction

Let 𝔤 be a simple complex Lie algebra of type 𝐴, 𝐷 or 𝐸, let 𝑘 ∈ ℂ ∖ ℚ, and let 𝑓 ∈ 𝔤 be any
nilpotent element. Quantum Hamiltonian reduction 𝐻𝑓0 is a braided tensor equivalence be‐
tween the category of finite direct sums of affine Weyl modules 𝕍𝑘𝜆 , induced from finite-di‐
mensional simple 𝔤-modules of dominant integral highest weight 𝜆, and the category of finite
direct sums of their reductions as 𝑊𝑘 (𝔤, 𝑓)-modules. The reduced category is rigid, with
𝐻𝑓0 (𝕍𝑘𝜆∗ ) dual to 𝐻𝑓0 (𝕍𝑘𝜆 ), where 𝜆∗ labels the dual finite-dimensional representation.
Reference: Creutzig–Dhillon–Nakatsuka, The Kazhdan–Lusztig category of W-algebras of simply-laced Lie algebras
at irrational levels (arXiv:2603.04667) (2026), Theorems 1.1 and 5.2; §§2.1–2.2.

OPEN PROBLEM

Nonlinear derived duality and exceptional products

For universal principal 𝑊3 and 𝑊4 over ℂ, construct a polarized completed curved dual, de‐
fine the null classes on smooth complexes and contramodules, and prove that the twisting ad‐
junction induces an equivalence after localization. The comparison must preserve the vacuum
and intertwine quantum Drinfeld–Sokolov reduction; its extension to rational simple quo‐
tients remains a separate question. For 𝑊𝑘 (𝔰𝔩4 ) with 𝑘 + 4 = ±3/2, ±2/3, determine the re‐
maining products, associative formality and the orders of the two local torsion summands.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Irrational W₃ sector cohomology

For 𝐾 ∈ ℂ ∖ ℚ and dominant integral 𝔰𝔩3 labels, the explicit paired 𝑊3 BRST complex has
zero cohomology away from the corrected partner labels. On the partner, its cohomology is
one-dimensional in each of degrees 0,3,5,8 and zero otherwise. The proof uses the nonzero
primitive-cover transgression from the Jantzen connecting map and the unique on-shell cores‐
olution column; the bottom differential is its transferred projection. This is a graded-vectorspace theorem. It does not construct sector multiplication, a two-row vertex algebra, a braidreversed equivalence, arbitrary-module derived Hom or a rational-level extension.

Reference: Gautam–Toledano Laredo, Meromorphic tensor equivalence for Yangians and quantum loop algebras
(arXiv:1403.5251v6), Publications Mathématiques de l’IHÉS 125 (2017), 267–337, §§2.5–2.6, 6.2–6.5.

78

OPEN PROBLEM

Elliptic fields with their global coefficients
For a complex elliptic curve 𝐸, extend scalar bar–cobar duality to matrix-valued meromorphic
exchange kernels with a chosen period bundle, and construct compatible state fields and colli‐
sion operations over 𝐸. The construction must specify the allowed polar divisors, the transla‐
tion action, the period holonomies and the tensor completion, and prove the exchange and as‐
sociativity identities on those same carriers. Comparing with an elliptic quantum vertex alge‐
bra requires identifying its classical state space and its elliptic modulus throughout the
deformation.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Matrix trigonometric fields in the multiplicative ring
In characteristic zero, the homogeneous matrix RTT construction gives integral divided fields,
exchange, level-changing coproducts, and cut residues at every finite formal precision. Its spec‐
tral values lie in the completed multiplicative ring 𝑘[𝑥 ±1 , (𝑥 − 1)−1 ], with 𝑥 = 𝑒 𝑧 . Already at
second precision, the displayed form does not belong to the additive Laurent-polynomial ring.
A common rational, trigonometric, and monodromic theory must therefore retain the value
ring, translation, and positioned tensor maps.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Binary Ward data do not determine a charged field family

For the translated rational-current module with 𝜅𝜆𝜈 ≠ 0, one selected inserted primary admits
the specified binary construction on every polynomial second input and for every current
mode. Its free scalar and shift data can give either an ordinary Laurent example with trivial full
turn or the normalized Kummer and Gaussian examples in their larger coefficient algebras.
Translation and binary Ward commutators alone therefore do not force the Gaussian mon‐
odromy. Fields of arbitrary inserted descendants, vacuum normalization, coherent charge fam‐
ilies and ternary collision identities remain separate constraints.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Necessary conditions for full RTT recognition
In characteristic zero, at nonzero deformation parameter and rank at least two, the fundamen‐
tal tensor square in the specified full RTT category is absolutely simple and has only scalar en‐
domorphisms. The constant RTT coefficient equation is an identity over arbitrary coefficient
algebras; it does not force commutativity. Any compatible Laurent presentation through an
exact faithful monoidal fibre must send the fundamental exchange to a polynomial matrix
with polynomial inverse and determinant one. These are necessary conditions. A full noncom‐
mutative cocycle, additive translation frame and comparison on every finite comodule remain
unconstructed.

79

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Cubic elliptic fields on all algebraic jet states
For the fixed elliptic two-torsion kernel on the algebraic differential polynomial state space
𝑃 = Sym⁡( ⨁𝑚 ≥ 0 𝔰𝔩(2𝑚) ), the local/formal state-field map is constructed through ℂ[ℏ]/(ℏ4 ).
It retains the original midpoint second-order symbol and 𝐾 = 𝑟02 /8 − 𝑟0′ /2, together with the
exact admitted normalized all-state cubic exchange. The four-factor and product-pole argu‐
ments give actual generating locality with a collision bound independent of spectator polyno‐
mial degree and jet orders, and reconstruction gives weak associativity on all polynomial states
with independent jets and full outputs. Admitted exchange retains finite meromorphic state
span per input, vacuum, shifts, period laws, unitarity and both cubic hexagons. Every admit‐
ted skew shift-covariant differential biderivation 𝑁, including nonlinear or jet-valued genera‐
tor outputs, is also realized by 𝛿𝑆3 = 𝑁, 𝛿𝑌3 = −𝑀𝑧 𝑁/2. The exact midpoint/endpoint
gauge preserves the declared recipients. This is a finite-precision local/formal theorem: the
common global coefficient category, native supported collision/cut/unit maps, KZ/associator
comparison, quartic all-polynomial extension and all orders remain separate. The normalized
zero homogeneous exchange correction does not solve the distinct quartic Yang–Baxter
equation.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

When wheel defects appear

Over a field 𝑘 of characteristic zero, let 𝛾 be a unit times 𝑔(𝑍1 − 𝑍2 ) on 𝔾𝑎 , or a unit times
𝑔(𝑍1 /𝑍2 ) on 𝔾𝑚 , where 𝑔 ≠ 0, 𝑔(0) ≠ 0 in the multiplicative case, and 𝛾(𝑍1 , 𝑍2 ) is coprime
to its reverse; either exchange sign 𝜀 = ±1 is allowed. Over an algebraic closure, let 𝑟 be the least
length of a root sequence of 𝑔, with repetitions allowed, whose sum is zero in the additive case
or whose product is one in the multiplicative case; set 𝑟 = ∞ if there is none. Then 𝑟 ≥ 3, both
wheel-supported defects of the quadratic-to-shuffle comparison vanish in weights 𝑚 < 𝑟, and
both are nonzero in every weight 𝑚 ≥ 𝑟. If 𝑟 < ∞, the weight-one-generated shuffle algebra is
neither quadratic nor Koszul for its weight grading.
New assembly of known results · Antecedents: Neguţ (2022); Neguţ–Sala–Schiffmann (2021); Dotsenko–Feigin–
Reineke (2021); Dotsenko–Feigin–Kucharski–Reineke (2024).

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

One-loop Hall currents and their coproduct

Over 𝑅 = ℚ[𝜂], take the zero-potential one-loop Hall algebra 𝐵 = ⨁𝑛 ≥ 0 𝑅[𝑥1 , …, 𝑥𝑛 ]𝑆𝑛 ,
with loop weight −𝜂, first input the subrepresentation, no rank shift and unaveraged shuffle
kernel (𝑣 + 𝜂)/𝑣. Its Cartan extension 𝐴 = 𝐵 ⋊ 𝑅[𝑝0 , 𝑝1 , …], where 𝑝𝑟 acts on weight 𝑛 by
multiplication by ∑𝑛𝑖 = 1 𝑥𝑖𝑟 , is faithfully realized by rational creation and occupation currents
over the family of all finite sets of algebraically independent sites. Cartan dressing gives a unital
algebra map Δ𝑧 : 𝐴 → (𝐴 ⊗𝑅 𝐴)((𝑧 −1 )), conjugate to separation of the sites, with
(𝜀 ⊗ 1)Δ𝑧 = id, (1 ⊗ 𝜀)Δ𝑧 = 𝑒𝑧𝑇 and shifted coassociativity: for three blocks at
(𝑥 + 𝑧 + 𝑤, 𝑦 + 𝑤, 𝑡), compose rationally and then expand at 𝑤 = ∞, followed coefficientwise
by 𝑧 = ∞. The comparison holds at 𝜂 = 0 as well as at nonzero parameters, and joint faithful‐
ness uses the whole family of finite site sets.
New assembly of known results · Antecedents: Kontsevich–Soibelman (2011); Tsymbaliuk (2021); Maillet–Sánchez
de Santos (2000); Latyntsev (2021); Jindal–Kaubrys–Latyntsev (2026).

From Hall currents to chiral fields

For the zero-potential one-loop quiver over ℚ[𝜂], construct a state–field correspondence on a
carrier supporting collision maps, and compare its operations with Hall convolution and the
Cartan-dressed spectral coproduct. Prove the resulting comparison on module categories, in‐

termine the coefficient lattices and support conditions needed for analogous constructions.

Completed and quantum celestial duality
For the two adjoint towers of a simple complex gauge Lie algebra, construct a completion and

taining the vacuum and all polynomial fibre degrees. Extend the quantum current and ghost

local observables of an independently quantized self-dual gauge theory on twistor space. A fi‐
nite-on-input principal-parts operator must be promoted to an algebra-valued operation on
the chosen completed carrier before it can define the required coproduct and module tensor
product.

CONDITIONAL

Reflection and the first surface correction

Consider one-flavour noncommutative Chern–Simons theory on ℝ𝑡 × ℂ2 , with
[𝑧1 , 𝑧2 ]∗ = 𝑐M , surface 𝑡 = 𝑧2 = 0, fixed classical surface currents, current level and universal
or finite-rank boundary condition for the surface algebra, and logarithmic flux odd in the loop
parameter ℏ. Assume a perturbative defect quantization exists with coincident-point countert‐
erms compatible with 𝑡 ↦ −𝑡, and that its Ward equations are reflection covariant with solu‐
tions unique modulo changes that are the identity on the classical currents. Then the first-or‐
der surface deformation class vanishes. If the currents and source coupling themselves admit a
reflection-equivariant choice, their first-order coefficients vanish in that choice.

OPEN PROBLEM

Preparing a gravitational state from the bulk
Construct regulated half-solid-torus integrals for real three-dimensional Einstein gravity in a
fixed asymptotically anti-de Sitter solid-torus sector, with an invertible real coframe, Brown–
Henneaux boundary conditions and a fixed boundary map, whose mixed pairings converge as
both regulators are removed independently. Prove that the resulting vectors satisfy the
Virasoro Ward identities, temperature evolution and physical sewing, have the
normalized modular vacuum amplitude, and use the same stress charge as the local bulk ac‐
tion. This requires an independently defined gravitational integral and its comparison with
the boundary representation.

Resolved root jets and selected normal comparisons
In the regulated half-space theory, the bare order-𝜒, cubic-field operation has strong ordinary
ℎ/ℎ and 𝑠/𝑠 limits with independent holomorphic jets of total order at most two, including
ℎ′ ⊗ ℎ′ . The actual source 𝐵1 ⊗ 𝐵1 , 𝐵1 = span⁡(ℎ, ℎ′ , 𝑠, 𝑠′ ), carries the required square and
grouped Hom differential. First-normal 𝑠/𝑠 restores its relative 𝑊ℎ term. Selected first-normal
ℎ/ℎ comparisons have separate bare-relative and complete-endpoint limits; individual end‐
point summands need not converge. Uniform tangential overlap holds for √𝜀 ≤ 𝑎 ≤ 𝑎0 on
ordinary ℎ/ℎ jets of total order at most two, one-normal ℎ/ℎ and ordinary 𝑠/𝑠. It does not
give a full independent-height-ratio collar or differentiated 𝑠/𝑠 overlap. Coupled level varia‐
tion acts on every constructed entry. The forget-normal map is noninjective by an explicit al‐
lowed Hom boundary, rather than a correction-invariant obstruction. The earlier unary lim‐
its, differential-stable algebraic source and tree second-normal formula remain. The full ana‐
lytic admissible class and correction quotient, all normal 𝐵1 operations, controlled higher
compositions, full height-ratio collar and independent physical stress comparison remain

OPEN PROBLEM

Positivity on arbitrary compact intervals

For the complete Weil quadratic form 𝑄 of the Riemann zeta function, prove 𝑄(𝑔) ≥ 0 for
every compactly supported complex 𝑔 ∈ 𝐿 2 (ℝ), interpreting the value outside the
finite-energy domain as +∞. The finite support-length bound does not provide this unbound‐
ed family of inequalities. The finite-matrix criterion retains the complete residual and tail
terms. Fourier and Schur reductions do not establish the required signed bounds at every
window.

OPEN PROBLEM

From the quantum obstruction to a defect algebra
For a cyclic holomorphic Chern–Simons theory on ℂ3 with its standard holomorphic volume
form and a chosen curve defect, construct the cyclic open-string dg or 𝐴∞ algebra, the cou‐
pling to transverse jets and a morphism from the local field-theory deformation complex to

cient and completion spaces.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Cubic compatibility for full retained-point current morphisms
For invariant cubic current systems on a complex surface, let 𝐴 be the prescribed unary map
and let ℓ𝜃, ℓ′ 𝜃′ be the central cubic tensors. A filtered vacuum-preserving morphism of the full
retained-point systems exists exactly when 𝐴 is a Lie map and ℓ𝜃 = ℓ′ 𝐴 ∗ 𝜃′ . The system retains
collisions, vacant-position maps, permutations, partitions, and their mixed coherences. The
map Sym⁡(𝐴) supplies a full morphism when these conditions hold. Lower correction terms
have the stated differential image, while full morphisms between fixed invariant-cubic fibres
have zero supported residual. Finite stages bound total particle, central, and Koszul weight to‐
gether. An independent mixed bulk, a singular-field realization, and an automorphism classifi‐
cation require further constructions.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Finite-jet cubic currents in free elliptic reduction
In free elliptic reduction on the fixed six-field carrier, confined cubic distributional limits, the
central de Rham primitive and raw eight-face cube lift remain. The specified one-lower-row
incoming 𝜂/𝑠 relation module now admits arbitrary finite independent holomorphic right
state jets, all anchors and fixed smooth joint coefficients, retaining the actual coefficient differ‐
ential. The third Green-pair derivatives are transferred by 𝜕𝑈 + 𝜕𝑉 before the annular esti‐
mates; the unrepaired differentiated moment estimate is not used. Separate marked-object
rows give the all-top 𝑠 and bare 𝜂 source equations and all joint faces at this scope. Critical mo‐
ments are signed, and high compact-anchor jets still need integration by parts rather than a
universal absolute-moment bound. The canonical cubic cone and contraction at every defined
contact order are available from the specified source. The full relative comparison still requires
broader polynomial and exterior history words, arbitrary intermediate outputs, the cubic
compact endpoint and independent projected, contact, localization and harmonic arrows
with fixed lower faces. Exactness in the full de Rham carrier does not imply exactness in the
smaller supported Dolbeault complex.

The raw output gauges from unweighted midpoint coordinates agree under nested mergers.

Two nested midpoint gauges differ
For the universal polynomial rank-one Heisenberg Chevalley–Eilenberg complex over a charac‐

This is a failure of the unweighted midpoint choice; it does not exclude weighted coordinates or

FALSE

A holomorphic vertex operator algebra is determined by its central charge, torus character,
ribbon category and scalar weight-one pairing.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Equal scalar data, different current brackets
The complex lattice vertex operator algebras 𝑉𝐸8 ⊕︎ 𝐸8 and 𝑉𝐷16+ are strongly rational and holomor‐

phic, have central charge 16, ribbon category of finite-dimensional complex vector spaces, and the
same full character 𝐸8 (𝜏)/𝜂(𝜏)16 , where here 𝐸8 (𝜏) denotes the normalized weight-eight
Eisenstein series. Their nondegenerate symmetric weight-one pairings are isometric over ℂ in di‐
mension 496, but their weight-one Lie algebras are 𝔢8 ⊕︎𝔢8 and 𝔰𝔬32 , with two simple ideals and
one, respectively. The simple pole of the current product distinguishes them.
