current/invariant-form formulas.

OPEN

Open problems
Comparing fields, configurations and duals
A comparison must preserve the operations needed to compose, collide and represent its objects.
OPEN PROBLEM

Realize completed duals by fields

Over ℂ, extend completed curved mode bars and twisting cochains for universal affine algebras of
finite-dimensional simple Lie algebras and universal Virasoro algebras at fixed levels and charges to
full ordered configuration diagrams. Construct both binary orders and their collision extensions,
with local vacuum, derivatives, exchange, unit and counit, and prove continuity on an explicitly de‐
fined completion; then compare the transported tensor with an independently constructed field or
module tensor.

OPEN PROBLEM

Ordered geometry and holomorphic-topological factorization
For a smooth separated complex curve 𝑋, specify coefficient and completion hypotheses under
which the ordered collision construction compares with factorization algebras on 𝑋 × ℝ that are lo‐
cally constant in the real direction. Construct the comparison with interval invariance and Weiss
descent, preserving differential-module coefficients, collision attachments, both unit structures and
local vacuum insertions; an equivalence of configuration function algebras alone does not supply
these data.

103

OPEN PROBLEM

A common spectral and curved-module comparison

For the nonzero-level Heisenberg algebra over ℂ, compare the curved-contramodule transform and
the coinvariant transform into 𝐷(ℂ[𝑎0 ]-Mod⁡) on one enhanced category of smooth complexes.
Construct the comparison independently of either inverse equivalence and preserve the vacuum,
logarithmic connection, collision maps and marked nilpotent charge extensions. Beyond strict dg
bialgebras and a change of connection marked by a translation-invariant degree-one element, a gen‐
eral singular curved comparison must also define and justify its weak equivalences.

PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Ordinary chiral reconstruction over classical parameter schemes
Let 𝐴 be a fixed ordinary unital Beilinson–Drinfeld chiral algebra on a smooth separated complex
curve. Its ordinary coefficient-family categories admit finite-map, collision, and insertion functors
over arbitrary classical parameter schemes. Their tensors are those of ordinary locally presentable
complex-linear categories, with Zariski descent. Separated fusion and evaluation retain the actual
inserted vacuum and normalized empty algebra, and the resulting reconstruction returns 𝐴. The
theorem fixes 𝐴. Derived or stable enhancements, varying nonflat algebras, physical comparisons,
and arbitrary fusion equivalences remain separate.
PROVED AND INDEPENDENTLY CHECKED

Symmetric integration with identity exchange
For a flat vertex algebra over a characteristic-zero coefficient algebra, with identity exchange, and a
punctured affine line, the explicit algebraic symmetric Cousin, cobar and Spencer diagram has allsurjection counits, its shuffle product and full-complex comparison homotopy, with the marked
vacuum retained and the empty unit separately adjoined. Its exhaustive direct-sum arity filtration
has fixed-arity coinvariant graded pieces. Eulerian projectors give an additive splitting and degenera‐
tion at the first page, with no multiplicative-splitting or completed-limit assertion. The vacuum tan‐
gent class in degree −1 is nonzero, graded-central and square-zero. Scalar total-cohomology formu‐
las retain the stated punctured-curve sign-isotypic hypothesis; free-translation and Heisenberg for‐
mulas also retain their separate fixed-arity input. General exchange, the non-separated-atlas and
proper-support comparison, negative cohomology of the previous carrier, curvature and continu‐
ous duality remain open. Lax monoidality alone decides neither equivalence nor non-equivalence
of the bar/de Rham comparison.

Fusion and higher operations
Finite representation categories and calculations on charge tops provide inputs to comparisons on com‐
plete modules.

104

OPEN PROBLEM

Nonabelian affine line fusion
For a finite-dimensional nonabelian Lie algebra over a characteristic-zero field with invariant level
form 𝜅 and invertible 𝛽 = 𝜅 + Kill⁡/2, compare the moving affine centralizer, the
restricted bar construction in the transverse line direction and line fusion on modules induced from
finite-dimensional charge tops. The comparison must specify weight and length completions and
preserve all induced states, nonlinear translation homotopies, products and three-module coher‐
ence. Extend the secondary operations on affine cochains to configuration-indexed operations with
coherent boundary action.

OPEN PROBLEM

A nonlinear module duality

For the universal regular 𝑊3 algebra over discrete ℂ at any central charge, construct a localized
equivalence between smooth modules and curved contramodules over its full bar dual, with an ex‐
plicit null class and compatible completion. Compare this equivalence with quantum Drinfeld–
Sokolov reduction; the algebraic bar construction and smooth-module resolutions alone do not
give the localized equivalence.
OPEN PROBLEM

Continuous Virasoro fusion on completed inputs

For real 𝑐 > 1, extend the existing four-completed-input collar sewing and its natural typed spectral
associators to a category representing the full trivalent Ward functor. Construct input spaces stable
under all admitted repeated insertions, compatible inverse domains and normalized categorical unit
triangles. Retain 𝐻, the independent label pentagon and the stated energy margins where used, and
preserve the separate geometric tree theorem at fixed 𝑐 > 25. The collar theorem and rapid complet‐
ed-vacuum identities do not establish these general categorical properties or arbitrary interior and
higher-arity sewing.

OPEN PROBLEM

Hall products, coproducts and spectral exchange

For a quiver moduli problem over ℂ and an oriented equivariant cohomology theory with an ex‐
plicit coefficient ring, compare the extension-correspondence product with a shuffle product on
one coefficient lattice. Define the localized direct-sum coproduct and chamber-dependent
stable-envelope maps on that same lattice, with all denominators, completions and geometric-tospectral parameter maps explicit, and prove their compatibility. A field realization must also extend
these operations across their collision loci.

Independent bulk theories and line defects
The bulk comparison must use observables and defects constructed from the bulk theory itself.

105

OPEN PROBLEM

Transport and marked extensions in the abelian theory
At nonzero complex Heisenberg level, construct a supported endpoint functor using either oppo‐
site graph transport or the reverse braided Fock category, with compatible framing and all finite de‐
scendants. Extend the comparison to marked nonsplit vacuum extensions when those are included
in the source category, and construct the mixed-operation map to derived vacuum endomorphisms
in that same category. Separately identify regulated exponential line insertions with the
supported source complexes through an endpoint or excision map in a topology that controls cap
removal.

OPEN PROBLEM

A nonabelian bulk–boundary–line theorem

For perturbative holomorphic-boundary 𝔰𝔩2 Chern–Simons theory on ℂ × ℝ≥0 , normalize
(𝑒, 𝑓) = 1, (ℎ, ℎ) = 2, take 𝜅0 ≠ 0 and coefficients ℂ((ℏ)), with boundary affine level 𝜅0 /ℏ − 2.
Construct supported maps from independently quantized bulk observables to derived endomor‐
phisms of the boundary vacuum and from finite-dimensional Wilson representations to boundary
modules. Prove that the same maps preserve normalized secondary operations, endpoint couplings,
line fusion and their higher coherences on common coefficient and completion domains.
OPEN PROBLEM

Higher-dimensional punctures and quantum obstructions

For a cyclic local field theory over ℂ with a defect, construct a map from its complex of local defor‐
mations to the defect deformation complex that carries the quantum obstruction to the corre‐
sponding defect obstruction. In the algebraic punctured-affine models in complex dimensions two
and three, retain 𝑅Γ(𝔸𝑑ℂ ∖ {0}, 𝒪) as a dg algebra, or transfer every higher product together with an
explicit 𝐴∞ quasi-isomorphism. Compare the normalized local operations and supports before
identifying an anomaly or a spectral coproduct.

States and gravitational sewing
An algebra of observables and a fusion category leave the preparation of a state undetermined.
OPEN PROBLEM

A state prepared by an independent gravitational bulk
For perturbative three-dimensional Einstein gravity with negative cosmological constant, in a fixed
topological sector with invertible coframe, construct an independently renormalized bulk ampli‐
tude on a region with a cut. Identify its state space and observable action with the boundary con‐
struction, prove compatibility with fillings and sewing on common analytic domains, and derive
the normalization of the cut measure from the bulk. The comparison must retain the boundary
conditions and finite counterterm prescription rather than infer a state or a universal charge shift
from Virasoro symmetry alone.

106

E S S AY

Essay
The comparisons depend on which operations and domains each construction retains.

Ordered composition and independent comparisons
R A E E Z L O R G AT

T

he Heisenberg current gives a basic example. Its singular product has a double pole with coefficient
equal to the level times the vacuum. A unital field map to scalars would have to preserve that pole.
Scalar fields have no singular products. The current algebra therefore has a vacuum but no scalar
augmentation at nonzero level.
Associative chiral Koszul duality retains this distinction. It studies multiplication, modules, and sin‐
gular collision with the order of the inputs fixed. A complex on configurations records the loci where op‐
erators meet. A field algebra records the corresponding coefficients. A module category records actions
and fusion. A comparison must specify the maps between these objects and the operations they preserve.
Ordinary chiral operations on a smooth curve impose a strong restriction. Strict associativity forces
both triple composites to vanish. A local vacuum that normalizes one side then forces the whole module
to vanish. Coherent associative constructions require a larger coefficient system. The Lie–commutative
theory supplies enveloping examples, while the general associative field comparison remains a separate
problem.
Ordered configurations have collision maps and cuts into consecutive blocks. A residue retains the
principal part and its normal derivatives. A cut gives a map from the whole configuration to its blocks. In
the connected augmented coefficient category, bar and cobar are inverse equivalences. A reduced word at
n positions has length at most n. This finite arity filtration does not remove the need to specify mode
completion or continuous duality.
For identity exchange, a separate symmetric algebraic diagram now supplies all-surjection counits, the
product, full-complex homotopies and an additive arity splitting. Its vacuum tangent is central and
square-zero. The scalar and free-translation formulas retain their specific inputs. General exchange, the
comparison with the non-separated atlas and continuous curved duality remain additional problems; the
new diagram is not an automatic equivalence of all those recipients.
The empty convolution unit and the local vacuum have different supports. The diagonal tensor has
its own dualizing unit. Atlas-to-word restriction can discard a nonzero separated summand. Curvature
records the failure of a chosen scalar splitting to preserve multiplication. These data remain part of the
duality even when an underlying complex or its cohomology forgets them.
Several field realizations are explicit. They include polynomial free currents, curved universal qua‐
dratic-linear-scalar algebras, marked charged jets, and paired Gaussian surface fields. Native rank-two
matching exists at the first quantum precision, while polar fields exist at the next precision. The remain‐
ing native comparison must identify composite marks, actual evaluation, both rooted actions, and every
required collision square.
The forward coefficient problem shows why a nonzero extension class is insufficient. An enlarged jet
quotient retains the actual class and global regular products. Its derived vacuum branches also exist. Fixed
singleton types can still force an occupied collision to vanish, and fixed products can retain the wrong
curvature. A successful successor must change the permitted domain or category while preserving the
original markings through an explicit comparison.
The transverse geometry has positive comparisons at several levels. Analytic configuration functions,
finite-pole conormal coefficients and native ray evaluations retain their established domains. Independent
rooted forests now have actual relative operations with both occupied vacua. The unrestricted scalar cur‐
rent complex is now computed with three escaping summands in addition to the two forests. Their nor‐
mal coordinates act invertibly, while the forest sum is characterized by simultaneous normal local nilpo‐
tence. The scalar vacuum evaluation has exactly that forest image and the escaping cokernel, so its old
global quasi-isomorphism assertion is false. A successful native extension must carry this calculation to
107

state coefficients and all collision maps while retaining the marked normal torsion. Adding a uniform
normal-order bound changes the problem. A separate three-position construction now uses two actual
contact images to form a cofiber and obtain the marked normal-linear comparison at invertible level. It
preserves the full old first cohomology and removes only the two level-weighted forest modules from sec‐
ond cohomology. Both polar vacua and the nonsplit extension survive, but the comparison kills a non‐
zero source class and has no homotopy left inverse. Independent all-position native origin, completed do‐
mains and the unscaled zero-inclusive map remain open.
Affine bulk cochains have algebraic state operations with the prescribed product and shifted sec‐
ondary pairing. The full two-copy projected fields agree on all algebraic states. At invertible shifted form,
the ghost-jet secondary is the average of the endpoint extractions. A geometric bulk comparison must also
construct supported operation complexes, boundary actions, and their collision homotopies.
The available centres solve different representing problems. Convolution centres and rooted vacuum
endomorphisms exist. Two-colour actions now have rooted collision homotopies, compatible pure-wall
nullity and common-winding vanishing. A constant singleton with the specified global propagating inser‐
tion also has a mixed-source binary filler retaining one prescribed cut and both collision branches. The ex‐
terior example still has a two-dimensional family of final fillers. Uniform-source data, comparisons be‐
tween independent cuts and coherent higher choices remain before full recognition. Algebraic nonrepre‐
sentability does not decide every physically specified action space. In the separate Heisenberg base-change
problem, surjective base change is now equivalent to the full tame cocycle subcategory defined by an
acyclic generated ideal. The full inclusion remains open, including higher homotopy. A functorial incom‐
ing tame refinement would suffice; relative PBW is one possible route. This partial result does not estab‐
lish the full localized-action comparison.
Filtered vacuum composition now has an all-level ambient model with its opposite order retained.
The shifted filtration controls repeated zero-energy ghosts. An explicit all-arity map compares the bar and
Chevalley models, and signed transposition gives the contragredient vacuum corner. Ordinary composi‐
tion changes to the complementary level. The natural finite-energy exact category misses ambient third
extensions, so it cannot supply every intrinsic comparison.
The reflected Virasoro comparison is available at every complex charge on the full subcategory of
bounded-below smooth complexes with jointly nilpotent cohomology. Its augmentation is the restricted
contragredient vacuum, and its ordinary endomorphism algebra is formal exterior on one degree-three
class even at nonsimple charges. The actual vacuum-to-contragredient map gives a multiplicative endo‐
morphism comparison with a nonzero cone at nonsimple charges; at simple charges the normalized map
is invertible. Matching these two marks does not determine the full unbounded null class, native collision
functor or physical state.
Algebraic collision coherence extends beyond individual associators. The fixed-contraction rational
gauge retains its literal four-position pentagon. Horizontal lifting, fixed-deletion strict units, finite subdi‐
vision comparisons and full-bar permutohedral evaluation families act on a faithful regular module. An
explicit algebraic cobar map is a retraction of shuffle, and the other composite has a relative polynomial
algebra homotopy to the identity. These operations are finite on each algebraic input. Strict coalgebra re‐
traction and arbitrary completed-dual comparison remain different questions. A native realization must
still match the actual vacuum and endpoint operations, tensorator, connection and higher faces.
Line fusion depends on the module domain. Finite Weyl sectors have explicit cochain, counit, ex‐
change, and associator comparisons. Ordered completed Heisenberg products preserve the admitted qua‐
si-isomorphisms through two recursive completion levels. Fixed finite trees retain exactness on their ad‐
mitted inputs. General third-level strictness, intrinsic leg recovery, common chamber transport, and geo‐
metric module units remain open.
The abelian physical example fixes a variance issue. State transport and operation transport are inverse
transposes, so their specified loop phases differ. A physical endpoint functor must use the corresponding
opposite transport or reverse braiding. The coefficient-native singleton category retains bounded compo‐
sition, units and finite duals. Its ordinary-derived point kernel is now constructed as local cohomology at
zero charge, while a separate hybrid diagonal coring retains all algebraic charged complexes. Finite separat‐

108

ed higher Wick maps and chosen interior primitive transport also exist. These constructions keep their
distinct tensor and weak-equivalence categories. The full geometric cut actor, common completion, non‐
split left endpoint and physical sewing still require their own comparisons.
Rational, multiplicative, elliptic and monodromic coefficients impose different analytic requirements.
The elliptic branch now has an actual local/formal cubic state-field map on all algebraic polynomial jets,
with the original midpoint lower fields and exact admitted all-state exchange. The four-factor argument
and uniform generating collision bound supply locality and weak associativity. Exact midpoint-to-end‐
point transport and admitted nonlinear biderivation corrections preserve that finite-precision construc‐
tion. A common global coefficient category, native supported operations, marked KZ and associator com‐
parisons, and quartic or all-order extensions remain separate.
Configuration arity and deformation order are independent. An all-finite family of collision homo‐
topies does not give the fifth coefficient of a quantum exchange. In continuous formality, a quartic ob‐
struction forces higher classical source operations. Proper-domain homotopies remain valid, while the
common quantum comparison and its coherent inverse require further construction.
Surface theories provide an all-arity paired Gaussian realization and a precise native cubic criterion.
For full retained-point morphisms, the unary Lie map must preserve the central cubic tensor. This criteri‐
on includes collisions, vacancies, permutations, and mixed coherences. It does not construct the indepen‐
dent interacting bulk or classify all automorphisms.
Free elliptic reduction has confined cubic current limits and an explicit central de Rham primitive.
Integration by parts retains distributional limits where high compact-anchor jets destroy absolute mo‐
ments. The specified incoming relation rows now admit arbitrary finite holomorphic right jets, after the
required third-pair derivative transfer. Marked all-top and bare-eta rows retain their actual source equa‐
tions and joint faces. The canonical cubic cone and contraction are available. The full relative map still
needs broader history words, the compact endpoint and independently prescribed projected, contact, lo‐
calized and harmonic arrows.
Gravity requires analytic estimates on an actual operation diagram. The ordinary cubic-field opera‐
tion now has two independent holomorphic jets and a differential-compatible square on the stated firstjet source. Selected first-normal comparisons retain separate bare-relative and complete-endpoint limits,
and specified tangential overlaps are uniform. Coupled level variation must differentiate the whole con‐
structed family. These results do not give separate limits for each endpoint summand. The full analytic
admissible class and its correction quotient, all normal operations on that source, independent-height-ra‐
tio collar, higher compositions and physical stress comparison remain to be constructed.
Continuous Virasoro sewing retains the adopted local theorem above charge one and the geometric
tree theorem at fixed charge above twenty-five. A later construction now admits four completed inputs
on a common entire-energy space over the five collar strips. Its actual flip identities and the independent
label pentagon give collar coherence under the retained boundary hypothesis. Typed spectral associators
have inverses on the same output energy scales. Full integer-mode Ward covariance and both rapid com‐
pleted-vacuum placements also hold on their stated domains. A resolved nested norm retains its interme‐
diate multiplicity label; integrating that label is a different operation. Point-label evaluation, collisionclosed input domains, a Ward universal property, all unit triangles and general interior or higher-arity
sewing remain open.
A separate one-screening torus family now identifies the actual positive partner measure with its com‐
plete Bergman Hilbert space and exact continuous kernel. The corrected proof uses a uniform integrable
inverse-square-root height bound; the older unweighted domination claim is false. With the fixed-cocycle
index theorem and the prescribed torus-stack normalization, the exact trace follows. In the original realexternal-momentum domain this establishes only \(c=33\), \(P_0=0\), \(h_0=4/3\), with trace \
(5/144\). The current generic route still imports the all-level torus AGT representation and assumes ac‐
tual positivity, regularity, weighted regulator control and an independently justified kernel-covariance in‐
put; these hypotheses remain open here. The generic sphere, c25/c28 metric comparison and physical
gravitational-state construction remain separate.

109

The wider programme retains independent Hall, brane, and higher-dimensional problems. Hall
products, localized coproducts, and stable envelopes need comparison on one oriented coefficient theory.
Numerical Igusa formulas do not determine primitive brackets or cyclic states. Pure-open obstructions
coexist with positive cotangent and open–closed theories. A defect comparison must carry the actual
quantum obstruction into the independently defined defect operations.
The arithmetic branch has finite-window positivity for the complete Weil form and integral
normal/Frobenius constructions. Global positivity requires all compact windows. An arithmetic field re‐
alization requires the full trace identity, both endpoint actions, normal collisions, and every prime-power
contribution. Finite positive matrices and altered positive damping do not supply those maps.
An independently prepared gravitational state is a further construction. Its amplitudes must select the
boundary representation, normalization, observable action, and sewing domain. A character, determi‐
nant norm, or algebraic cap does not fix all this data. The comparison must begin with the bulk ampli‐
tude and retain its boundary conditions and counterterm prescription.

A realized vacuum diagram assigns a quasi-coherent coefficient sheaf
to the vacuum at every finite configuration, with its finite-map, unitinsertion and disjoint-fusion comparisons. Admissibility here re‐
quires the stated coherent comparison isomorphisms, no nonzero
sections supported on collision diagonals, and normalization of the
empty coefficient as an algebra with its true unit. These are geometric
hypotheses on the full diagram.

The additional scalar-current families denoted 𝐸 and 𝐹 in the celes‐
tial gauge presentation. Their coupling modifies the current prod‐
ucts, and cancellation of the resulting quartic trace tensor is the con‐
dition used in the finite quantum quotient.

sum. Specifying a class in this group retains more information than
specifying only a numerical Chern character.

lows arbitrary coproducts. Neither is automatically the whole de‐

Algebraic cotensor

For a right 𝐾-comodule 𝑀 and a left 𝐾-comodule 𝐿, 𝑀 □𝐾 𝐿 is the

Becker-coacyclic positive quotient
For a curved ghost contramodule, remove the negative ghost coeffi‐
cients by the contramodule quotient, including all their summation
relations; the result is a dg module over the positive-mode cochain al‐
gebra. It is Becker-coacyclic when its Hom complex into every dg
module with graded-injective underlying module is acyclic.

Arakawa–Kuwabara–Möller formal sheaf

The ℏ-adically complete sheaf of vertex superalgebras on Hilb𝑁 ⁡(ℂ2 )
constructed by local framed 𝔤𝔩𝑁 BRST reduction of adjoint and fun‐
damental symplectic bosons with fundamental fermions. Its quo‐
tient modulo ℏ is the jet sheaf of the corresponding reduced super‐
manifold. Formal sheaf cohomology, finite ℏ-quotients and torus-fi‐
nite numerical specialization are distinct constructions.

Beltrami differential

A Beltrami differential locally has the form 𝜇(𝑧) 𝑑‾
𝑧/𝑑𝑧 and de‐
scribes a deformation of complex structure through ‾
𝜕𝑓 = 𝜇𝜕𝑓. For
‖𝜇‖∞ < 1, normalized solutions are quasiconformal maps. Compact
support and surface-group periodization specify the actual families
used in the closed-surface counterexamples.

Arithmetic jet

For a prime 𝑝, a 𝛿-ring has a Frobenius lift 𝜑(𝑎) = 𝑎𝑝 + 𝑝𝛿(𝑎); the
iterates 𝑎, 𝛿𝑎, 𝛿2 𝑎, … are arithmetic jet coordinates. In a 𝑝-torsionfree ring the Frobenius lift determines 𝛿.

Bergman space and fundamental-domain trace

The Bergman space 𝐴 2 (𝔻) here is the Hilbert space of square-inte‐
grable holomorphic one-forms on the disc. For bounded operators
commuting with a torsion-free cocompact group whose disc quo‐
tient is a closed surface of genus 𝑔 ≥ 2, integrating the diagonal ker‐
nel over one fundamental domain gives the finite group trace, nor‐
malized here by 𝜏Γ (1) = 𝑔 − 1. This trace is defined on the equi‐
variant algebra, and need not agree with an ordinary trace on the full
Hilbert space.

Associated lattice
For the holomorphic vertex operator algebras considered here, the
Heisenberg charges occurring with the vacuum module of the com‐
mutant form a positive-definite even lattice 𝐿. The corresponding
lattice vertex algebra is the mutual commutant of the Heisenberg
commutant.

A Bers slice parametrizes marked quasi-Fuchsian structures while
holding the conformal structure of one boundary component fixed.
It realizes the other Teichmüller variable in a bounded domain of
holomorphic quadratic differentials, with the surface-group equi‐
variance fixed.

Big and generated shuffle algebras
The big shuffle algebra contains the full polynomial or Laurent-poly‐
nomial coefficient space in every weight, with its prescribed permuta‐
tion character and shuffle product. The smaller algebra is the subal‐
gebra generated by weight-one coefficients; wheel conditions can
make this inclusion proper.

Cofree conilpotent coalgebra and Taylor components
The algebraic cofree conilpotent symmetric coalgebra on a graded
vector space 𝑊 is the direct sum of its finite symmetric powers, with
coproduct obtained by all splits of the inputs. A coderivation or coal‐
gebra map is determined by its projections from those powers to 𝑊,
called its Taylor components. On 𝑆 𝑐 (𝑍[3]), a degree-one square-zero
coderivation encodes the shifted Lie operations of arity 𝑛 and degree
4 − 3𝑛 on 𝑍.

Browder bracket
For an 𝐸3 -algebra, the fundamental class of the two-point configura‐
tion sphere defines a binary operation of cohomological degree −2,
called the Browder bracket. Its sign depends on the chosen orienta‐
tion of that sphere.

Common-cutoff sewing
Sewing in which all pieces and their common boundary use compati‐
ble regulators before any regulator is removed. The boundary identi‐
ties and their higher compatibility maps must hold for that single
regulated construction.

cap
A vector or continuous functional assigned to a boundary compo‐
nent filled on one side. In the positive boundary trace model, a cap at
inverse temperature 𝛽 is √𝑤(𝑃) 𝑒 −𝛽𝐻𝑃 /2 ; pairing caps yields the cor‐
responding sewn amplitude.

compact smooth kernel carrier
At each fixed number of fields, observables are completed projective
bornological tensor powers of compactly supported smooth forms,
represented by compact smooth kernels in the corresponding num‐
ber of variables. The direct sum over field number remains algebraic,
so an observable has only finitely many field-number components.

Cartan extension
The extension of a Hall or shuffle algebra by commuting moment
operators 𝑝𝑟 . In the one-loop example, [𝑝𝑟 , 𝑏𝑠 ] = 𝑏𝑟 + 𝑠 , and dressing
the coproduct by the corresponding Cartan series makes separation
of sites compatible with these actions.

Charge top
The finite-dimensional coefficient module from which an affine or
Heisenberg module is induced. For a horizontal affine top, 𝔤 acts and
positive loop modes act trivially; generalized Weyl induction permits
finite jets killed by a sufficiently high power of 𝑧.

Co-vacuum
For a chosen splitting of loop modes into nonnegative and negative
powers, the co-vacuum is the module induced from the trivial repre‐
sentation of the negative-mode subalgebra. The usual vacuum is in‐
duced from the nonnegative modes; their derived invariants and
coinvariants can behave differently.

Consecutive-cut convolution
For a sequence of coefficients on ordered configurations, sum over
decompositions into consecutive blocks, take the external tensor
product of the block coefficients, restrict to the locus where different
blocks are disjoint, and apply derived direct image back to the full
configuration space. Its unit has coefficient 𝑘 at the empty configura‐
tion and zero elsewhere.

Constant ghosts

Determinant-torsion classes

For the formal Hilbert-scheme vertex sheaf 𝒟, these are
𝜅𝑟 = 𝑇 𝑟 𝛽(𝜙𝜓), where 𝛽 is the connecting map for multiplication by
ℏ and 𝜙𝜓 is the classical contraction of the two fundamental fermi‐
ons. The first class has a logarithmic cocycle representing the first
Hodge Chern class of the tautological determinant line. Their gener‐
ated positive-degree subalgebra is killed by ℏ; its exterior signs come
from sheaf degree, while the intrinsic parity is even.

The constant ghosts are the energy-zero generators 𝑐0𝑎 of the polyno‐
mial affine Chevalley–Eilenberg complex. They form the subcom‐
plex 𝐶 ∗ (𝔤; 𝑘) = Λ𝔤∗ , with its Chevalley–Eilenberg differential and,
for a nondegenerate invariant form, the degree-minus-two inverseform bracket.

Constant profinite labels

For a profinite set 𝑆, the constant profinite formal scheme over ℤ𝑝
has coordinate algebra 𝐶(𝑆, ℤ𝑝 ) of continuous functions, with its 𝑝-

Discriminant gluing
A positive-definite even lattice 𝐿 has finite quadratic group
𝐷 = 𝐿∗ /𝐿, with 𝑄(𝑎) = (𝑎, 𝑎)/2 mod ℤ. A discriminant gluing
identifies this form with the negative of the form labeling the com‐
mutant’s invertible modules, so that the paired summands have inte‐
gral conformal weight; for the stated strongly rational holomorphic
class with positive nonvacuum commutant weights, they extend the
tensor-product vertex algebra. In a higher-genus amplitude, one such
identification acts on every handle.

adic topology. A label on a test algebra may vary across its idempo‐
tent components; it is not required to be one globally constant ele‐
ment of 𝑆.

contact terms
Contact terms are distributional contributions supported where in‐
sertion points coincide. The formal source statements fix them
through the Cauchy Green operator and the chosen cutoff prescrip‐
tion; separated-point kernels alone do not determine an arbitrary
extension.

divided convolution bracket
The convolution bracket on multilinear maps whose target bracket is
the graded commutator divided by ℎ. In the polynomial two-matrix
setting that commutator is divisible by ℎ, so the operation is defined
without inverting the parameter.

Coupling modulo total translation
A degree-one coupling 𝐼 obeys its Maurer–Cartan equation after
quotienting states by the image of total translation:
𝑄𝐼 + 21 𝐼(0) 𝐼 = 𝐷tot 𝐻. Since a total derivative has zero zero-mode, this
identity can produce a square-zero operator on coefficient modules.

divided Moyal bracket
For the Moyal product with parameter ℎ, the bracket
𝑏ℎ (𝑓, 𝑔) = (𝑓 ∗ℎ 𝑔 − 𝑔 ∗ℎ 𝑓)/ℎ is polynomial in ℎ on polynomial
inputs, because the numerator is divisible by ℎ. At ℎ = 0 it becomes
the Poisson bracket.

The matrix-current presentation uses 𝑅(𝑥) = 1 − ℏ𝑃/𝑥, where 𝑃 in‐

Divided-power algebra

Γ𝑅 (𝑡1 , …, 𝑡𝑚 ) denotes the free divided-power algebra over 𝑅. Its ele‐

Curved contramodule
A module-like object over a complete curved algebra, equipped with
coherent summation operations for coefficient families tending to
zero. Its degree-one connection squares to the action of the curva‐
ture; a topology on the contramodule itself is additional data.

Empty unit and local vacuum
The empty unit has scalar coefficient 𝑘 at the empty configuration
and zero elsewhere; a local field vacuum is marked identity-field data
at occupied configurations, including vacuum insertions and princi‐
pal-part identities. The word/cut tensor unit Ωcut 𝐽, obtained by co‐
bar from the dualizing coefficient coalgebra 𝐽, is a separate object. Its
tensor-unit property neither supplies a scalar vertex character nor
identifies it with a field-vacuum vector.

curved point dual
A curved differential graded algebra has a degree-one derivation 𝑑
and a degree-two element ℎ with 𝑑 2 = [ℎ, −] and 𝑑ℎ = 0. For the
universal mode algebras here, its continuous Koszul-dual cochains
retain the central cocycle as ℎ; the completion and the module local‐
ization are part of the duality data.

Cyclic state
A state or trace functional compatible with cyclic rotation of com‐
posable operations and with the proposed sewing maps. A scalar gen‐
erating series alone does not specify such a functional or its prepara‐
tion by a cap.

Endpoint extraction

formal source

After gauge transport of a two-position pairing, choose an output
coordinate 𝑢𝑡 = 𝑡𝑧1 + (1 − 𝑡)𝑧2 and normal coordinate
𝑤 = 𝑧1 − 𝑧2 . Extracting the 𝑑𝑤 coefficient and then normal de‐
rivative order zero gives a state operation. The endpoint extractions
use 𝑡 = 0 and 𝑡 = 1; their average is distinct from extraction at the
midpoint.

A source treated through its formal Taylor coefficients, which are
multilinear functionals of smooth compactly supported test inputs.
A finite-parameter source family is a power series with zero constant
term in those parameters. Convergence after assigning numerical pa‐
rameter values is a separate assertion.

energy completion

All linear maps from finite exterior powers of the Lie algebra into the
coefficient module, without a support or continuity restriction. For
unbounded derived Hom, product totalization retains every compo‐
nent contributing to a fixed total degree.

Full algebraic cochains

For a module with generalized energy eigenspaces 𝑋[ℎ] , the energy
completion is ∏ℎ 𝑋[ℎ] , replacing the direct sum over energies by a
product. For a complex, completion term by term is a separate opera‐
tion from totalization.

For 𝑍 = ⨁𝑒 ≥ 0 𝑍𝑒 with energy-preserving differential, an energy re‐
traction onto 𝐴0 = 𝑍0 consists of inclusion 𝑖, projection 𝑝, and an

For a nonnegatively energy-graded complex, an energy-additive oper‐

finite Weyl filtration

full radical descent

A finite Weyl filtration is a finite filtration whose successive quotients
are affine modules induced from finite-dimensional 𝔤[[𝑧]]-modules
killed by a sufficiently high power of 𝑧. Its exact structure comes
from exact sequences of smooth affine modules.

A three-point form descends through a singular intermediate module
when it annihilates its entire contravariant radical, including every
descendant of every null vector. Sewing through the simple quotient
requires this property for both three-point forms.

finite-energy domain

fusion lattice

Energy-additive operation

A fusion lattice near shifted level 𝜅0 is the module over ℂ[[𝜀]], with
finite free generalized-energy blocks, obtained by deforming fusion
to 𝜅0 + 𝜀 and embedding it into the generic fibre over ℂ((𝜀)).
Associators and braidings preserve this lattice and specialize by re‐
duction; it need not be the lattice supplied by a generic Weyl basis.

For the two commuting positive Virasoro actions, this is the union of
the spectral subspaces on which 𝐿0 + ‾
𝐿0 is bounded above. Each
mode acts on this common invariant domain before Hilbert
completion.

finite-word vacuum-mode algebra

Geometric roof pairing

The actual unital image of finite products of vertex-mode operators
acting on the algebraic direct sum of the vacuum algebra's confor‐
mal-weight spaces. It has no completion in the mode indices; its
curved bar does not by itself classify modules for a completed vertex
algebra.

A roof represents a map after localization by a diagram 𝐴 ← 𝑅 → 𝐵
whose left arrow is an allowed equivalence. The geometric roof pair‐
ing uses a resolving object and its independently defined configura‐
tion operations to compare the two pairings. Its nullhomotopy on
separated configurations is retained as part of the comparison data.

Grunsky operator

Ind-category and algebraic sector sum

Ind⁡(𝒞) is the category obtained by adjoining filtered colimits to 𝒞.

For a normalized univalent map 𝑓, the mixed coefficients of
log⁡((𝑓(𝑧) − 𝑓(𝑤))/(𝑧 − 𝑤)), or its differentiated kernel, define the
Grunsky operator on a Bergman Hilbert space. In the finite-genus
setting here it maps conjugate holomorphic one-forms to holomor‐
phic one-forms, is equivariant for the fixed surface group, and has
norm less than one. Hilbert–Schmidt and ordinary trace-class prop‐
erties must be distinguished from a finite fundamental-domain trace.

The Virasoro field object here is a direct sum of simple sectors: each
state has finite sector support, even though a field output is complet‐
ed in conformal weight inside its finitely many possible sectors.

Injectivity radius
The injectivity radius at a point of a curvature-minus-one hyperbolic
surface is the largest radius on which the exponential map is injective.
A disc of any smaller radius embeds isometrically. The large-disc ob‐
struction uses a radius bound at one point, not a lower bound at
every point of the surface.

Internally graded Hom

After choosing a Cartan subalgebra of 𝑉1 , its currents generate a
Heisenberg vertex algebra. The Heisenberg commutant consists of
the states whose fields commute with these currents, equivalently
those annihilated by their nonnegative modes.

Hermitian holomorphic kernel and positive type

Lattice genus and hyperbolic genus

Heisenberg commutant

A lattice genus consists of lattices isometric over ℝ and over ℤ𝑝 for
every prime 𝑝. For the strongly rational holomorphic class with
strongly rational Heisenberg commutant considered here, a hyper‐
bolic genus consists of the holomorphic extensions with that com‐
mutant, an associated lattice in the same lattice genus, and the com‐
patible discriminant gluings. Isomorphism classes include the identi‐
fication of Cartans up to conjugacy.

A Hermitian holomorphic kernel 𝐾(𝑋, 𝑍) is holomorphic in 𝑋, anti‐
holomorphic in 𝑍, and satisfies 𝐾(𝑋, 𝑍) = 𝐾(𝑍, 𝑋). It is of positive
type if ∑𝑖, 𝑗 𝑎𝑖 𝐾(𝑋𝑖 , 𝑋𝑗 )𝑎𝑗 ≥ 0 for every finite set of points and co‐

efficients. Such a kernel is a Gram pairing of Hilbert-space evaluation
vectors.

Hessian obstruction
The adjoint Chevalley–Eilenberg class represented by the cocycle
Ω𝐻 built from derivatives of the Hamiltonian Hessians. Its nonvan‐
ishing prevents an algebraic two-cochain from cancelling that partic‐
ular cubic residual.

Local simple-current line categories
The finite-length, grading-restricted module categories obtained by
local lifts through the simple-current boundary extensions in the
abelian mirror construction of Ballin–Creutzig–Dimofte–Niu. In
the two-flavour example the charge maps are 1 ↦ (1,1) and 1 ↦ (1,−1);
the extension and tensor unit are retained, so these are smaller than
all smooth current modules.

Identity level
In the finite fusion-operator example, identity level means that the
level endomorphism 𝜂 : 𝐻 → 𝐻 is id𝐻 ⁡. It is a specified datum of
that example, distinct from the choice of bicharacter and comodule
braiding.

Loop-space factor

The additional factor 𝐻𝑏 (Ω𝐺; ℂ) in positive-integral derived affine
fusion inside the universal-affine category. Passing to the simple-ver‐
tex-algebra fusion output applies its augmentation and retains loop

Modular torus fibration

The proposed complex threefold maps properly and flatly to ℙ1 , with
smooth fibres complex two-tori, multiple fibres of multiplicities
three and four at zero and one, and a reduced non-normal crossing fi‐
bre at infinity whose normalization is the degree-six del Pezzo sur‐
face. The uniformization, marked period lattice, finite quotient fill‐
ings, logarithmic transforms and toric cusp gluing are geometric hy‐
potheses; the underlying smooth topology is a separate question.

marked collision
A collision operation includes the map obtained when field inser‐
tions meet a distinguished module insertion, together with the vacu‐
um insertion and all normal derivatives. Retaining all marked colli‐
sions asks for these maps at every finite number of points and their
composition identities.

Normal convolution
The arithmetic normal carrier retains independent displacements 𝑣
and their iterated 𝛿-coordinates around each arithmetic point.
Addition of displacements gives a coproduct Δ on their coordinate
algebra 𝐻𝑟 , and normal distributions multiply by
(𝜆 ∗ 𝜇)(𝑓) = (𝜆 ⊗ 𝜇)Δ(𝑓). The finite-weight distributions are

Marked vacuum extension
A marked vacuum extension is a short exact sequence

Normal-cup product
The operation on sheaf cohomology obtained by combining the
Čech cup operation with the vertex-algebra (−1)-product on local
sections. Associativity must be proved on the relevant subspace; the
determinant-torsion theorem establishes it on the displayed
subalgebra.

Mass of a genus
The mass is the sum of reciprocal orders of the specified finite sym‐
metry groups over the isomorphism classes in the genus. For a lattice
it uses the full orthogonal group; for the holomorphic vertex opera‐
tor algebras here it uses the finite image of the Cartan-preserving au‐
tomorphism group on the associated lattice, retaining the kernel of
its action on the discriminant group.

Meromorphic tensor comparison

normalized Virasoro sphere block

Natural comparison maps between tensor products that vary mero‐

the products are defined. The Drinfeld products here depend on that

The four-point block obtained by contracting the two highest-vec‐

Metaplectic convention
The metaplectic group is the double cover of a real symplectic group
on which square roots of the determinant automorphy factor can be
chosen coherently. Half-integral-weight theta and Eisenstein series
use this cover together with the Weil representation; the compatible
factors make the combined summand independent of the chosen lift.

Null class
A class of complexes declared to become zero in a derived localiza‐
tion. For curved or topological duality, this class must be defined
through the actual functors and topology; ordinary acyclicity need
not give the required localization.

odd-zero-mode kernel

polynomial Fock module

The kernel of the zero-mode operator of an odd vertex field. In the
Hilbert-scheme example the field is 𝜒− = 𝜙𝛾, and its zero mode anni‐
hilates exactly the image of the boundary BRST algebra.

At nonzero Heisenberg level 𝜅, a polynomial Fock module with zeromode coefficient space 𝐸 is 𝐸[𝑥1 , 𝑥2 , …], using finite polynomials.
Creation modes multiply by 𝑥𝑚 , annihilation modes act by 𝑚𝜅𝜕𝑥𝑚 ,
and the zero mode acts on 𝐸.

Ordered collision construction

polynomial ghost algebra

Coefficient data over configurations with ordered labels, together
with their extensions across collision diagonals and compatibility un‐
der cuts and mergers. Keeping a single word order with consecutive
cuts and gluing all order charts are distinct constructions.

The graded-commutative algebra generated by odd variables dual to
Lie-algebra generators and their derivatives, with the Chevalley–
Eilenberg differential. Polynomial means finite support in those vari‐
ables; it does not permit an unrestricted product over the generator
labels.

ordinary vertex-algebra module
An ordinary module has diagonalizable 𝐿0 , finite-dimensional
weight spaces, and weights bounded below in finitely many integral
cosets. A grading-restricted generalized module permits generalized
𝐿0 -eigenspaces under the same finiteness and lower-bound
conditions.

Plumbing and section-valued sewing

Plumbing glues two coordinate discs by 𝑧𝑤 = 𝑞, with 𝑞 the sewing
parameter. A formal sewing amplitude is expanded in finitely many
such parameters using graded states and their duals. An amplitude
continued as a section has the prescribed conformal-anomaly line
and coordinate transition laws; it need not be a globally defined
scalar function on moduli.

gree one, and the model uses polynomial functions, algebraic sym‐
metric algebras and ordinary quasi-isomorphisms.

Positive PBW weight and the finite-support dual
For the one-position affine bar, the positive PBW weight assigns
2𝑛 + 1 to 𝑗𝑛 = 𝑥−𝑛 − 1 and 𝑐𝑛 , and adds weights in monomials and
bar words. Completion allows infinite sums as this positive weight
increases. The continuous graded dual has finite support in the com‐
pleted weight direction, so an algebraic inverse with an unbounded
fixed-energy word tail can fail to act on it.

Polarization and diastasis

The holomorphic polarization of a real-analytic potential Φ is a
function 𝐹(𝑧, 𝑤) whose diagonal is Φ(𝑧). Its diastasis is
𝐷(𝑧, 𝑤) = Φ(𝑧) + Φ(𝑤) − 𝐹(𝑧, 𝑤) − 𝐹(𝑤, 𝑧), which removes the
ambiguity of adding the real part of a holomorphic function to Φ.
The normalization at a base point fixes the remaining holomorphic
gauge used in the kernel.

A half-space propagator built from the heat kernels
𝑔𝑠 (𝑡 − 𝑡 ′ ) ∓ 𝑔𝑠 (𝑡 + 𝑡 ′ ), using the minus sign for Dirichlet and plus

Relative cofibration

A primitive for the Ward or master-equation term on the comple‐
ment of the singular insertion or divisor. Sewing requires its exten‐
sion and boundary values to agree across the cuts used to join
regions.

A relative cofibration of dg operads retains a specified source operad
while permitting homotopically free extensions of its operations. In
the concrete cellular presentations used here, generators are adjoined
in well-ordered stages and each new differential lies in earlier stages;
retracts give the general cofibrations. This is the hypothesis that al‐
lows an action to be extended while keeping already specified opera‐
tions fixed.

Punctured-flow identity

Relative smooth localization

A compactly cut off lift 𝑣 of a meromorphic vector-field germ 𝑢 gen‐

𝜕𝑣 + 𝑣𝜇′ − 𝜇𝑣′ . The Virasoro
punctured-flow identity relates the source functional along succes‐
sive such flows to ordered insertions 𝑒 𝑠𝑇[𝑢] and their central anomaly
factor. It determines the contact terms for overlapping cutoffs.

Fix the positive-mode Lie subalgebra of a local mode algebra. The
semiderived smooth category is the homotopy category of smooth
complexes modulo those whose restriction to that subalgebra is coa‐
cyclic. Here coacyclic complexes form the smallest triangulated sub‐
category containing totalizations of short exact triples and closed un‐
der coproducts and retracts.

Strict dg bi-Galois object
A dg bi-Galois object is a dg algebra with commuting left and right
Hopf coactions, scalar coinvariants, and invertible left and right
canonical Galois maps. Strict means that these maps are actual dg iso‐
morphisms on the specified algebraic complexes. In the exterior ex‐
ample both Hopf algebras are 𝐾.

Restricted RTT Koszul cochains
For a positive finite-type RTT lattice 𝑀, the cochains are
𝒦(𝑀) = 𝑀 ⊗ˆ 𝐵, with differential 𝑑𝐵 + 𝜏𝑀 , where 𝐵 is the RTT
ghost algebra and 𝜏𝑀 is the universal twisting cochain evaluated on
the Yangian action. The tensor and mode sums use weightwise and ℏ
-adic completion, and sufficiently high mode evaluations lie in ℏ𝑁 𝑀
for every fixed 𝑁. Arbitrary-value full linear duals need not satisfy
this restriction.

Strictly plurisubharmonic potential
A real-valued potential is strictly plurisubharmonic when its
Hermitian Hessian (𝜕𝑖 ‾
𝜕𝑗 Φ) is positive definite. If the potential is real
analytic it admits a local holomorphic polarization; positivity of this
Hessian alone is weaker than positive type of its exponential kernel.

Rotation-finite sector
The subspace of vectors whose rotations around the boundary point
span a finite-dimensional space. In the boundary and endpoint com‐
plexes this selects the algebraic sum of rotation-weight components,
rather than an unrestricted product over energies.

Strong continuous dual
The vector space of continuous linear functionals, equipped with the
topology of uniform convergence on bounded subsets of the original
locally convex space. This topology retains convergence information
that the algebraic dual does not specify.

Smith exponent

For a Gram matrix over ℂ[[𝜀]] that is invertible over ℂ((𝜀)), the
Smith exponents are the powers of 𝜀 in its diagonal invariant factors.
Positive exponents describe the orders at which null directions ap‐
pear; all positive exponents equal to one means the derivative pairing
on the radical is nondegenerate.

Strong cut coalgebra
A coalgebra for consecutive-cut convolution whose coproduct for
each cut is an equivalence on the locus where the two blocks are dis‐
joint. Iterated cuts satisfy the coherent coassociativity identities.

Strong orientation data

Smooth mode module

A square root of the determinant line of self-extensions, together
with compatible square-root isomorphisms for direct sums and exact
triangles. The compatibility includes associativity over every actual
ordered filtration; it is additional structure beyond an isolated square
root.

A discrete module for a local mode algebra is smooth when every vec‐
tor is annihilated by all sufficiently high positive modes. The cutoff
may depend on the vector; a uniform cutoff, an energy grading and
finite-dimensional weight spaces are additional hypotheses.

spectral-label pentagon

Strongly rational and holomorphic vertex operator algebras

The pentagon identity for integral kernels that change the internal
representation labels in successive fusions. Its validity on label
Hilbert spaces does not alone construct a tensor product represent‐
ing the trivalent Ward maps.

A strongly rational complex vertex operator algebra is simple, selfcontragredient, rational, of CFT type 𝑉 = ⨁𝑛 ≥ 0 𝑉𝑛 with
𝑉0 = ℂ 1, and 𝐶2 -cofinite: the quotient by the span of 𝑎(−2) 𝑏 is fi‐
nite-dimensional. Rationality means complete reducibility of admis‐
sible modules. It is holomorphic when its only irreducible ordinary
module is itself.

Stable-envelope exchange
A stable envelope maps fixed-point equivariant cohomology into the
cohomology of a symplectic resolution after choosing a chamber and
polarization. Comparing the maps for two chambers gives an ex‐
change operator on that equivariant coefficient space.

This differs from inverting all quasi-isomorphisms of the underlying
ghost complexes.

Tensorator
A tensorator for a strong monoidal functor 𝐹 is a natural isomor‐

part of the fibre functor used to assemble the degenerate Virasoro
fields.

Uncurving mark
An uncurving mark specifies the change of differential used to com‐
pare a curved or marked module problem with ordinary complexes.
In the exterior example, 𝜆 ∈ 𝑘𝑟 changes 𝐷 to 𝐷 − ∑𝑖 𝜆𝑖 𝑁𝑖 ; the weak
equivalences are the maps that become quasi-isomorphisms under
that particular change.

Translation nullhomotopy
A translation nullhomotopy is a degree-minus-one operator 𝐾 with
𝑑𝐾 + 𝐾𝑑 = 𝑇, where 𝑇 is translation. This identity does not require
𝐾 to be a derivation of the native product. A separate homotopy be‐
tween two comparison maps has a different defining equation.

Unitary minimal charges

The unitary Virasoro minimal charges are 𝑐 = 1 − 6/[𝑝(𝑝 + 1)] for
integers 𝑝 ≥ 2; 𝑝 = 2 is the zero-charge endpoint. The positive mini‐
mal charges correspond to 𝑝 ≥ 3. Their appearance in the universal
positivity theorem does not by itself settle the fixed-genus kernel.

modules. With diagonal exceptional tensor and dualizing unit 𝐽, it is

then requires its own restricted or continuous dual and completion.

120

Vacuum polarization

Weight-preserving operadic augmentation

The splitting of Laurent current modes into the nonnegative modes
that annihilate the vacuum and the negative creation modes. The as‐
sociated ghost representation depends on this splitting and on the
finiteness needed for its determinant cocycle.

An operadic augmentation is a morphism from an operad to the
identity operad, which has only its identity operation in arity one.
For an integer-weighted operad it is weight-preserving when every
nonzero-weight element maps to zero and the identity has weight
zero. A strict algebra unit together with multiplication can obstruct
such an augmentation.

Ward maps and represented products
A Ward map satisfies the current or stress-tensor Ward identities at its
inputs and output; the rational construction includes an output in‐
sertion at infinity with restricted contragredience. A represented
Ward product is a module with a universal insertion map, so that
morphisms to an allowed output module are naturally the corre‐
sponding Ward maps. Its representing object is the Ward output;
scalar conformal-block identities or a pentagon on spectral labels
alone do not supply this universal property.

Weightwise complete lattice

For formal ℏ of weight −1, the completion has the form
𝑀 = ⨁𝑤 ∏𝑗 ≥ 0 ℏ𝑗 𝑀0 [𝑤 + 𝑗]. Boundedness below and finite type
mean respectively that 𝑀0 [𝑤] = 0 below a lower bound and that
each 𝑀0 [𝑤] is finite-dimensional; neither permits an unrestricted
product over all weights.

Weil–Petersson metric and form

Weak factorization system of categories

For harmonic Beltrami representatives on a closed hyperbolic sur‐
face, the full Weil–Petersson Hermitian pairing used here is
𝐻(𝜇, 𝜈) = ∫ 𝜇𝜈 𝑑𝐴. Its form is 𝜔WP = 𝑖𝐻𝑗 𝑘 𝑑𝑡𝑗 ∧ 𝑑‾𝑡 𝑘 . This nor‐

Such a system gives categories over finite configuration spaces, com‐
patible parameter pullbacks and external fusion on disjoint configu‐
rations, together with coherent unit and partition maps. In the weak
form used here, external fusion on the whole categories need not be
an equivalence, although the specified vacuum comparisons for sur‐
jections and disjoint fusion are required to be invertible.

malization fixes the scale 𝑐/(24𝜋) in the projective-immersion
criterion.

Weil–Petersson quasicircle
A quasicircle is the image of the circle under a quasiconformal map.
A normalized exterior univalent map belongs to the Weil–Petersson
class when its Grunsky operator is Hilbert–Schmidt. This universal
welding domain is distinct from the Teichmüller space of a fixed
closed surface.

Weak module
A module for a vertex algebra whose fields satisfy vectorwise lower
truncation and the vertex-module identities, with no prescribed con‐
formal grading, positive-energy bound or finite-dimensionality. For
the finitely generated current presentations used here, sufficiently
high modes kill each individual vector.

Wheel locus

For a scalar kernel 𝛾, draw a directed edge 𝑖 → 𝑗 at a configuration
when 𝛾(𝑍𝑗 , 𝑍𝑖 ) = 0. The wheel locus is the locus of configurations
with a directed cycle, or its image in the symmetric configuration
space; it can lie away from ordinary collision diagonals.
